# 27 — Affordable 35B-class fit matrix

Which recorded accelerators can hold a ~35B-class model, and what does the
cheapest one cost per unit of decode throughput.

> **Refresh note (docs/fit-matrix-oct14, Oct-18, this pass).** Re-ran `python tools/fit_matrix_35b.py` on local `main` `547e8ad` (236 accelerator records, 218 supply records, 4,667 benchmarks — #287 through #294 on top of the #286 matrix, which was itself stamped on `81b8382`). The selection rule still yields **159 qualifying / 43 priced / 116 unpriced** (69 of the unpriced fit 35B-A3B, 47 do not). Every priced cell was re-diffed against the generator: id, VRAM, bandwidth, the three fit flags, the 35B ceiling, the supply id, the price, the derived $/tok-s, and the order all match the table already below, so **the ranked table does not move** and no rank is renumbered. None of the eight commits adds an accelerator or a purchase price. What they add is two more offload-shaped MoE checkpoints, a 74B hybrid whose 4-bit packing sits between the 24 GB fit bar and the 48 GB stretch bar, a diffusion LM whose cache field is zero, and one more graph-capture offload paper measured on the same 35B-A3B class this ceiling is built for.
>
> - **SeqMoE says a 24 GB card holds 40% of Qwen3.6-35B-A3B-BF16 and still returns three quarters of full-load decode.** [[papers/seqmoe-graph-compatible-moe-offload]] measures batch-1 decode with the cache at 45% of experts (40% for Qwen3.6): **75.69%** of full-load throughput on Qwen3.6-35B-A3B-BF16, against 84.82% on Qwen3-30B-A3B-FP8, 79.20% on GPT-OSS-120B-MXFP4 and 76.65% on DeepSeek-V4-Flash, averaged to 80.22% in the abstract. Full-load means every weight resident, with pipeline parallelism when one GPU cannot hold them. The same section puts an eager offload at about 2% of full-load, so the gap this paper claims is the orchestration, not the bandwidth. Hardware in its Section 9.1 is three cards already in this table: [[accelerators/nvidia-rtx-4090]] (rank 22, 24 GB) with 120 GB host RAM over PCIe 4.0, [[accelerators/nvidia-rtx-5090]] (rank 12, 32 GB) with 120 GB over PCIe 5.0, and [[accelerators/nvidia-rtx-pro-6000-blackwell]] (rank 34, 96 GB) with 256 GB over PCIe 5.0. On the 4090 over PCIe 4.0 one layer of Qwen3-30B-FP8 compute overlaps **1.39** expert transfers, which is why a one-layer lookahead cannot hide the copy. The predictor itself is 120 MB, 228 MB, 112 MB and 223 MB on those four models, and the authors say the remaining miss (3.25% average, abstract hit rate 96.97%) is still the largest single piece of decode time. These are the authors' measurements; no independent tok/s is in the record, and Figure 6's absolute tok/s were not transcribed. Nothing here changes a fit flag: a `Y` is still "the 20 GB Q4 file fits," and 75.69% of full-load is a different question this table does not answer.
> - **Intern-S2-397B is the same 17B-active body as Qwen3.5-397B-A17B, and the file is what does not fit.** [[models/intern-s2-397b]] is 403.423B total / 17B active, 10 of 512 routed experts plus one shared expert, continued from [[models/qwen3-5-397b-a17b]]. 17/20 = **0.85×** the 20 GB the 35B ceiling divides by *(derived)* — closer to a dense read than Qwen3.6-35B-A3B's 3/20 = 0.15, and the ceiling still divides by the resident file. A 4-bit packing of the safetensors count is 403.423e9 / 2 = 201,711,500,000 bytes = 201,711,500,000 / 1,073,741,824 = **187.86 GiB** *(derived)*. That is past 24 GB, past the 48 GB the 70B column tests, and past the 96 GB of rank 34; it needs the 192 GB row ([[accelerators/apple-m2-ultra]], rank 33) or larger, or the offload the record describes. The notes derive KV on the 15 full-attention layers as 15 × 2 × 256 × 2 × 2 = **30,720 B/token**, the same figure the base record stores. 30,720/20,480 = **1.50×** the [[models/qwen3-6-35b-a3b]] geometry *(derived)*. 8,192 tokens is 30,720 × 8,192 = **240 MiB** *(derived)*, and the 4 GB the fit flag reserves holds 4 × 1,024³ / 30,720 = **139,810 tokens** *(derived)*. The record's `kv_cache_bytes_per_token` field is **15,360**, half the notes' arithmetic and not what this paragraph uses. No benchmark of this checkpoint lands on a card in §4b.
> - **DeepSeek-V4-Pro-0813 is a post-train of the 1.6T preview, not a smaller file.** [[models/deepseek-v4-pro-0813]] keeps the preview nameplate: 1,600B total / 49B active, 6 of 384 routed experts plus one shared expert. 49/20 = **2.45×** the 20 GB denominator *(derived)*, so the dense-equivalent ceiling *overstates* a 49B-active decode relative to the 3B-active model it was built for, and the file is the part that does not fit anywhere in the priced table as a single resident checkpoint. A 4-bit packing of the nameplate is 1,600e9 / 2 = 800,000,000,000 bytes = 800,000,000,000 / 1,073,741,824 = **745.06 GiB** *(derived)* — past the 512 GB M3 Ultra (rank 41). The record's own weights are the FP4/FP8 mixed release (safetensors metadata 1,650,497,936,906 parameters, dominated by I8), so the on-disk checkpoint is below that 4-bit-of-BF16 figure, and `kv_cache_bytes_per_token` stays null because the compressor layers do not collapse to one byte count. Six experts per token is narrower than the 8-expert routing the SeqMoE paper's overlap number was measured against. The card's Terminal-Bench 2.1 is 87.9, a vendor claim, and it is not a tok/s.
> - **Nemotron 3.5 Bolt is the 24 GB-class cut of the 120B Super VL, and the BF16 parent does not fit 24 GB.** [[models/nemotron-3-5-bolt-74b-a9b]] is 74B total / 9B active, 8 attention blocks out of 88. 9/20 = **0.45×** the 20 GB denominator *(derived)*. A 4-bit packing of the card's round 74B is 74e9 / 2 = 37,000,000,000 bytes = 37,000,000,000 / 1,073,741,824 = **34.46 GiB** *(derived)*: past the 24 GB the 35B fit flag tests (so a 24 GB card is still `-` for the file) and inside the 48 GB the 70B column tests. The card's recommended artifact is a separate 2-bit checkpoint it says fits in 24 GB; this record is the BF16 parent, and that quant is not a record here. KV on the 8 attention blocks is 8 × 2 × 128 × 2 × 2 = **8,192 B/token**, 8,192/20,480 = **0.40×** the Qwen3.6 figure *(derived)* — the same ratio the previous note computed for the 120B parent. 8,192 tokens is **64 MiB** *(derived)*, and 4 GB holds 4 × 1,024³ / 8,192 = **524,288 tokens** *(derived)*. The Mamba state is extra and is not in that KV number.
> - **K2-Horizon-MoVA's 4-bit file fits inside the 20 GB the ceiling divides by; its KV does not fit inside the 4 GB headroom.** [[models/k2-horizon-mova-36b-a4b]] is 37.445B total / 4B active, 8 of 100 routed experts plus one shared expert. 4/20 = **0.20×** the denominator *(derived)*. A 4-bit packing is 37.445e9 / 2 = 18,722,500,000 bytes = 18,722,500,000 / 1,073,741,824 = **17.44 GiB** *(derived)*, under the 20 GB weights budget, so a card the 35B column marks `Y` holds this file with room to spare. The cache does not: `kv_cache_bytes_per_token` is **196,608**, 196,608/20,480 = **9.60×** the Qwen3.6 geometry *(derived)*, because the Mixture-of-Values heads ride on top of ordinary GQA. 8,192 tokens is 196,608 × 8,192 = **1.50 GiB** *(derived)*, and the 4 GB headroom holds 4 × 1,024³ / 196,608 = **21,845 tokens** *(derived)* before the cache is the thing that spills. Terminal-Bench 2.1 at 58.6 is a vendor claim and not a tok/s.
> - **LLaDA2.2-mini is the one new checkpoint a 12 GB card can hold, and it is not an autoregressive decode.** [[models/llada2-2-mini]] is 16.256B total / 1.4B active, 8 of 256 experts. 1.4/20 = **0.07×** the denominator *(derived)*. A 4-bit packing is 16.256e9 / 2 = 8,128,000,000 bytes = 8,128,000,000 / 1,073,741,824 = **7.57 GiB** *(derived)*, inside the 12 GB of [[accelerators/intel-arc-b580]] (rank 2), which the 35B column marks `-`. `kv_cache_bytes_per_token` is **0**: the record is iterative block diffusion with `use_cache` false, so the 4 GB headroom is not a KV budget. A full bidirectional K+V buffer, which the notes say is *not* what the field records, would be 20 × 4 × 128 × 2 × 2 = **40,960 B/token** *(the notes' arithmetic)*. The card's own serving example is still SGLang at tensor-parallel size 8, because the diffusion block and the 128K context are the constraint, not the parameter count. No absolute tok/s is in the record.
> - **#294 measures the stretch model on the unpriced 48 GB card, and the prefill half of the same page.** [[benchmarks/exllamav2-quadro-rtx-8000-llama33-70b-5bpw-8k-tg]] is **10.43** decode tok/s on [[accelerators/nvidia-quadro-rtx-8000]] (48 GB, 672 GB/s, `Y/Y/Y`, 35B ceiling 672/20 = **33.6** tok/s *(derived)*, no purchase price, so it stays in §4) for Llama 3.3 70B EXL2 5.0 bpw at 8k, and [[benchmarks/llamacpp-b5508-quadro-rtx-8000-llama33-70b-q5km-8k-tg]] is **7.59** tok/s for the same card on Llama 3.3 70B Q5_K_M. The 70B ceiling is 672/40 = **16.8** tok/s *(derived)*; 10.43/16.8 = **0.62×** and 7.59/16.8 = **0.45×** that floor *(derived)*. Both sit under it, which is what a 5-bit file does against a ceiling built for a 4-bit 40 GB file, and neither is the 35B-A3B workload the ranking sorts on. The rest of the commit is batch-1 llama.cpp prompt processing, not decode. Qwen3 30B-A3B Q4_K_XL on the rank-11 3090 is **1,958.99** tok/s at 16k and **1,336.79** at 32k ([[benchmarks/hc-rankpp-rtx3090-qwen3-30b-a3b-pp-16k]], [[benchmarks/hc-rankpp-rtx3090-qwen3-30b-a3b-pp-32k]]). The rank-34 RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell runs the same model at **5,084.45 / 3,863.04 / 2,864.83 / 1,270.31** tok/s for 16k / 32k / 65k / 131k ([[benchmarks/hc-rankpp-rtxpro6000-qwen3-30b-a3b-pp-16k]], [[benchmarks/hc-rankpp-rtxpro6000-qwen3-30b-a3b-pp-32k]], [[benchmarks/hc-rankpp-rtxpro6000-qwen3-30b-a3b-pp-65k]], [[benchmarks/hc-rankpp-rtxpro6000-qwen3-30b-a3b-pp-131k]]). Prefill is not the memory-bandwidth roofline this table divides by, so none of those cells enters the $/tok-s ranking.
>
> - **#293's other benchmark cites do not move a byte.** The same commit attaches vendor scores to [[models/qwen3-6-35b-a3b]] (SWE-bench Verified 73.4, GPQA Diamond 86.0, MMLU-Pro 85.2) and to [[models/qwen3-8-27b]] (SWE-bench Verified 77.2), plus scores on checkpoints whose geometry the earlier notes already sized ([[models/veriloop-e2]], [[models/solar-open2-250b]], [[models/nemotron-3-5-super-vl-120b-a12b]], [[models/step-5-preview]], [[models/naive-n0-5-flash]], [[models/ornith-1-5-35b-a3b]], [[models/qwen3-5-122b-a10b]], [[models/qwen3-5-397b-a17b]], [[models/mimo-v2-6-pro-rl]], [[models/ling-3-1-flash]], [[models/beam-1-0-501b-a23b]], [[models/aliceai-foundation-80b-a3b-base]], [[models/kimi-k2-6]], [[models/mimo-v2-6-flash-rl]], [[models/nemotron-labs-dory1-4b]], [[models/ornith-1-5-9b]], [[models/xing4-0-29b-a4b]]). None of those edits changes `params_b`, `active_params_b`, or a KV byte count, and none is a measured tok/s, so none enters §4b. [[papers/specprefetch-window-aware-expert-prefetch]] was already on `main` before the #286 stamp; this pass does not re-derive it.

> **Refresh note (docs/fit-matrix-oct14, Oct-18, prior pass).** Re-ran `python tools/fit_matrix_35b.py` on local `main` `81b8382` (236 accelerator records, 218 supply records, 4,532 benchmarks — #286 on top of the #285 matrix, which was itself stamped on `84256e2`). The selection rule still yields **159 qualifying / 43 priced / 116 unpriced** (69 of the unpriced fit 35B-A3B, 47 do not). Every priced cell was re-diffed against the generator: id, VRAM, bandwidth, the three fit flags, the 35B ceiling, the supply id, the price, the derived $/tok-s, and the order all match the table already below, so **the ranked table does not move** and no rank is renumbered. #286 adds no accelerator and no purchase price. What it adds is a 120B vision-language hybrid whose 4-bit packing clears the 48 GB stretch bar and still fits a 96 GB card, with a KV cache smaller than the 35B-A3B the ceiling is built for.
>
> - **Nemotron 3.5 Super VL is a 12B-active read of a 120B file, and the file is the part that does not fit.** [[models/nemotron-3-5-super-vl-120b-a12b]] is 120B total / 12B active, hybrid Mamba-2 plus MoE plus attention, 22 of 512 routed experts plus one shared expert. 12/20 = **0.60×** the 20 GB the 35B ceiling divides by *(derived)* — the dense-equivalent ceiling understates a 12B-active decode by less than it understates Qwen3.6-35B-A3B's 3B (3/20 = 0.15), and it still divides by the resident file. The record's `params_b` is the card's round 120B, not a safetensors count, so a 4-bit packing is 120e9 / 2 = 60,000,000,000 bytes = 60,000,000,000 / 1,073,741,824 = **55.88 GiB** *(derived, 4-bit = 0.5 B/param)*. That is past the 24 GB the 35B fit flag tests and past the 48 GB the 70B column tests, and it is inside the 96 GB of [[accelerators/nvidia-rtx-pro-6000-blackwell]] (rank 34) and the 80 GB A100/H100 rows. The card says the other ~490 experts stay in RAM and only the 22 move per token, so the resident-file test and the per-token read are different numbers; this table's `Y` is the file. KV is the 8 attention blocks only: 8 × 2 kv heads × 128 × 2 × 2 = **8,192 B/token**, 8,192/20,480 = **0.40×** the [[models/qwen3-6-35b-a3b]] figure *(derived)*. 8,192 tokens is 8,192 × 8,192 = **64 MiB** *(derived)*, and the 4 GB the fit flag reserves holds 4 × 1,024³ / 8,192 = **524,288 tokens** *(derived)* before that headroom is the cache. A 128k window is 8,192 × 131,072 = **1.00 GiB** *(derived)*. The record says the Mamba recurrent state is the other memory term and is not in the 8,192. No benchmark of this checkpoint, so it does not enter §4b, and it does not move a fit flag: the 24 GB line is a weight test.

> **Refresh note (docs/fit-matrix-oct14, Oct-18, prior pass).** Re-ran `python tools/fit_matrix_35b.py` on local `main` `84256e2` (236 accelerator records, 218 supply records, 4,532 benchmarks — #280, #282 and #283 on top of the #281 matrix, which was itself stamped on `db47423`). The selection rule still yields **159 qualifying / 43 priced / 116 unpriced** (69 of the unpriced fit 35B-A3B, 47 do not). Every priced cell was re-diffed against the generator: id, VRAM, bandwidth, the three fit flags, the 35B ceiling, the supply id, the price, the derived $/tok-s, and the order all match the table already below, so **the ranked table does not move** and no rank is renumbered. None of the three commits adds an accelerator or a purchase price. What they add is a 37.4B MoE whose 4-bit packing fits inside the 20 GB the 35B column divides by, a 250B hybrid whose 4-bit packing does not, a CPU expert kernel whose own numbers are ratios and not tok/s, and the first four measurements on a 72 GB card §4 already lists as unpriced.
>
> - **K2-Horizon-MoVA-36B-A4B packs inside the 20 GB column, and its KV does not.** [[models/k2-horizon-mova-36b-a4b]] is 37.445B / 4B active, 8 of 100 routed plus one shared expert, and the first three layers are dense. 4/20 = **0.20×** the 20 GB the 35B ceiling divides by *(derived)*. A 4-bit packing of the safetensors count is 37.445e9 / 2 = 18,722,500,000 bytes = **17.44 GiB** *(derived, 4-bit = 0.5 B/param)*, under the 20 GB weight bar and under the 24 GB the fit flag actually tests. KV is the ordinary GQA formula, and the record says the MoVA value routing is not in it: 48 layers × 8 kv heads × 128 × 2 × 2 = **196,608 B/token**, 196,608/20,480 = **9.60×** the [[models/qwen3-6-35b-a3b]] figure *(derived)*. 8,192 tokens is 196,608 × 8,192 = **1,536 MiB** *(derived)*, and the 4 GB the fit flag reserves holds 4 × 1,024³ / 196,608 = **21,845 tokens** *(derived)* before the headroom is gone. 17.44 + 1.50 = **18.94 GiB** of weights plus that 8k cache *(derived)*, which is why a 24 GB card still holds the short context and why a 128k window does not. The card's Terminal-Bench 2.1 of 58.6 is a vendor claim against [[models/xing4-0-29b-a4b]] at 57.50, so it does not enter §4b. No benchmark, no rank.
> - **Solar Open 2 is the other shape: the active slice is small and the checkpoint is not.** [[models/solar-open2-250b]] is 250.288B / 15B active, 8 of 320 plus one shared expert, hybrid attention with KV only on 12 of 48 layers. 15/20 = **0.75×** that same denominator *(derived)* — closer to a dense 20 GB read than Qwen3.6-35B-A3B's ~3B, so the ceiling overstates it less and the card still cannot hold the file. A 4-bit packing is 250.288e9 / 2 = 125,144,000,000 bytes = **116.55 GiB** *(derived)*, past the 24 GB bar, past the 48 GB the 70B column tests, and past the 96 GB PRO 6000. Growing KV is the 12 softmax layers only: **49,152 B/token**, 49,152/20,480 = **2.40×** the Qwen3.6-35B-A3B geometry *(derived)*, and 8,192 tokens is 49,152 × 8,192 = **384 MiB** *(derived)*. The record's own check is that all 48 layers would be 4× that, 196,608 B/token, which is the quarter the card claims. The 36 linear-attention layers are a fixed recurrent state. LiveCodeBench v6 of 92.4 is a vendor claim. No benchmark, so it does not enter §4b, and it does not move a fit flag: the 24 GB line is a weight test.
> - **HiNa-MoE changes the CPU expert kernel. It does not change a ceiling.** [[papers/hina-moe-amx-general-layout]] keeps routed-expert weights in plain row-major and fuses the AMX layout transform into the gathered-token matrix, so one buffer serves the CPU path and the GPU path. The authors' own FFN kernel, geometric mean, averages **1.73×** over IPEX and **1.68×** over KTransformers (peaks 3.37× and 2.16×) and reaches **96%** of measured local bandwidth on a dual Xeon Gold 6430 (STREAM local 234 GB/s, cross-socket 95 GB/s, 95/234 = **0.41×** *(derived)*). End to end, batch 1, 128 in and 128 out, dual 6430 plus an [[accelerators/nvidia-rtx-a6000]]: decode averages **1.99× / 1.70× / 1.22×** over vLLM CPU-only, IPEX and KTransformers, and the record says the IPEX and KTransformers builds already contain the HiNa-MoE operators. No absolute decode tok/s is printed. Prefill on Qwen2-57B-A14B with GQA on the A6000 and experts on the CPU is **1,526.66** tok/s at 2K against 1,187.43 for SGLang plus KTransformers (**1.29×**, 1526.66/1187.43 *(derived)*) and **2,085.47** at 32K against 1,252.54 (**1.67×** *(derived)*). A6000 stays the unpriced §4 row at 768 GB/s (38.4 tok/s ceiling *(derived, 768/20)*) and the §4b cell at 131.2 tok/s. A kernel ratio on a host-RAM expert path does not move that row.
> - **The 72 GB PRO 5000 now has four numbers, and none of them is the 35B-A3B.** [[accelerators/nvidia-rtx-pro-5000-72gb-blackwell]] is already the §4 row at 72 GB, 1,344 GB/s, ceiling 1,344/20 = **67.2** tok/s *(derived)*, **Y/Y/Y**, no bare-card price — tied with the 48 GB sibling on bandwidth, with the extra 24 GB buying the stretch model and no tok/s. HOSTKEY's own Ollama table (quant, prompt length and output length unstated, confidence 0.55) is the first published set: deepseek-r1:14b **67.71** ([[benchmarks/hostkey-rtxpro5000-72gb-deepseek-r1-14b-ollama-decode]], 67.71/67.2 = **1.01×** the 20 GB floor *(derived)*), deepseek-r1:32b **32.89** ([[benchmarks/hostkey-rtxpro5000-72gb-deepseek-r1-32b-ollama-decode]], 32.89/84.0 = **0.39×** the 27B ceiling of 1,344/16 *(derived)*), gpt-oss:20b **149.38** ([[benchmarks/hostkey-rtxpro5000-72gb-gpt-oss-20b-ollama-decode]], 149.38/67.2 = **2.22×** *(derived)* — the wrong byte count for a ~21B MoE, same caveat the 4080/5080 note already uses), and qwen3-coder-next **74.34** ([[benchmarks/hostkey-rtxpro5000-72gb-qwen3-coder-next-ollama-decode]], 74.34/67.2 = **1.11×** *(derived)*). The page says that last model is about 51 GB, so the dense-equivalent read is 1,344/51 = **26.4** tok/s and 74.34/26.4 = **2.82×** *(derived)*, which is a calibration of a ~51 GB file, not a Q4 35B-A3B. The 48 GB sibling's §4b cell stays 188.8 at 4k on a stated Q4_K; these four are a different engine with the quant unpublished, so they are not averaged into that cell and they do not enter §4b. Still no purchase price, so the row stays in §4.

> **Refresh note (docs/fit-matrix-oct14, Oct-18, prior pass).** Re-ran `python tools/fit_matrix_35b.py` on local `main` `db47423` (236 accelerator records, 218 supply records, 4,528 benchmarks — #275, #276, #278 and #279 on top of the #274 matrix, which was itself stamped on `4f8ec2e`). The selection rule still yields **159 qualifying / 43 priced / 116 unpriced** (69 of the unpriced fit 35B-A3B, 47 do not). Every priced cell was re-diffed against the generator: id, VRAM, bandwidth, the three fit flags, the 35B ceiling, the supply id, the price, the derived $/tok-s, and the order all match the table already below, so **the ranked table does not move** and no rank is renumbered. None of the four commits adds an accelerator or a purchase price. What they add is a 27.8B dense hybrid that fits the same cards the 27B column already tests, a 2023 sub-1-bit expert kernel whose own end-to-end number is slower than the uncompressed baseline, 113 ranking-page cells that are a different Hardware Corner page from the ones §4b already quotes, and a draft-selection rule that cuts host-to-device expert copies rather than moving a bandwidth ceiling.
>
> - **VeriLoop E2 is the same 27.8B dense hybrid the 27B column already prices.** [[models/veriloop-e2]] is a fine-tune of [[models/qwen3-8-27b]]: 27.781B parameters, `active_params_b` equal to `params_b` (no `num_experts` key), 64 layers, KV **65,536 B/token**. 27.781/16 = **1.74×** the 16 GB the 27B-Q4 weight column divides by *(derived)*, so a Q4 packing of it is the workload that column is for, and the whole checkpoint is resident wherever that one is — the record says so, and nothing is offloaded to RAM. Its KV is 65,536/20,480 = **3.20×** the [[models/qwen3-6-35b-a3b]] figure *(derived)*, and 8,192 tokens is 65,536 × 8,192 = **512 MiB** *(derived)*. That is context cost, not a weight-fit change: the 20 GB bar is unchanged, and the card's GPQA / AIME / SWE-bench numbers are vendor claims, so they do not enter §4b.
> - **QMoE's kernel is a GEMV, and the paper's own end-to-end number loses.** [[papers/qmoe-sub-1-bit-compression]] stores a ternary routed expert in a 16-bit-codeword dictionary at under 1 bit per parameter. The isolated compressed matrix-vector product beats PyTorch bfloat16 cuBLAS by up to **35 percent** on the SwitchTransformer expert shapes, but the same weights decoded to 128 tokens are **under 5 percent slower** than the uncompressed baseline ([[gotchas/qmoe-sub-1-bit-gemv-only-pays-at-batch-1]]). The kernel is one warp per row and one codeword per step, so a second token on the same expert is a second launch while the baseline runs one matmul. Both numbers are the authors' own, on [[accelerators/nvidia-rtx-a6000]] and [[accelerators/nvidia-rtx-3090]] only, and the end-to-end comparison is against a simulated baseline that points every expert at one copy of the weights. The checkpoints are a custom dictionary format, not GGUF or GPTQ. A6000 stays the unpriced §4 row at 768 GB/s (38.4 tok/s ceiling *(derived, 768/20)*); the 3090 stays rank 11. Neither number is a decode tok/s, so neither enters §4b.
> - **#278 is a second Hardware Corner page, and it does not replace the cells §4b ranks on.** 113 new per-request decode rows from the December 9, 2025 ranking table (llama.cpp CUDA 12.8, Q4_K_XL, batch 1, 16K/32K/65K/131K). The commit skips any cell whose value was already stored, which is why these do not collide with the March per-card pages. They also do not agree with them. Qwen3-30B-A3B Q4_K_XL at 16k on this page: **113.84** on the 3090 ([[benchmarks/hc-rank-rtx3090-qwen3-30b-a3b-q4kxl-tg-16k]], 113.84/46.8 = **2.43×** *(derived)*) against the March page's 98.3; **121.88** on the 3090 Ti ([[benchmarks/hc-rank-rtx3090ti-qwen3-30b-a3b-q4kxl-tg-16k]], 121.88/50.4 = **2.42×**) against 121.9 on the March page and 98.25 on the older ranking ingest; **139.71** on the 4090 ([[benchmarks/hc-rank-rtx4090-qwen3-30b-a3b-q4kxl-tg-16k]], 139.71/50.4 = **2.77×**) against 139.2 in March and 109.57 on the older page; **120.12** on the 6000 Ada ([[benchmarks/hc-rank-rtx6000ada-qwen3-30b-a3b-q4kxl-tg-16k]], 120.12/48.0 = **2.50×**) against 120.1 in March and 104.88 on the older page. Same shape at 32k: 3090 **87.21** (87.21/46.8 = **1.86×**, [[benchmarks/hc-rank-rtx3090-qwen3-30b-a3b-q4kxl-tg-32k]]) against 58.8 in March. The two pages are different measurements of the same card and quant, so neither is averaged into the cell §4b already cites, and neither moves a rank. The page does add depths the March series does not have. [[accelerators/nvidia-rtx-5090]] (rank 12, $1,999, 89.6 tok/s ceiling): **141.63** at 16k (1.58×, [[benchmarks/hc-rank-rtx5090-qwen3-30b-a3b-q4kxl-tg-16k]]), **110.65** at 32k (1.23×), **76.56** at 65k (0.85×), **52.84** at 131k (0.59×) — all ÷ 89.6 *(derived)*. The floor is crossed between 32k and 65k, the same place the 3090 and the 6000 Ada already cross it. [[accelerators/nvidia-rtx-pro-6000-blackwell]] (rank 34, $8,650, same 89.6 ceiling) holds the 30B-A3B through 131k above the floor at 16k and 32k: **139.76 / 103.33 / 80.15 / 56.14** (1.56× / 1.15× / 0.89× / 0.63×, [[benchmarks/hc-rank-rtxpro6000ws-qwen3-30b-a3b-q4kxl-tg-16k]] and the 32k/65k/131k siblings) — the 65k cell is the first one under it. The same card's Llama 70B Q4_K_XL row is the first measured 70B-class cell on a priced 70B-fit part: **28.24** at 16k ([[benchmarks/hc-rank-rtxpro6000ws-llama-70b-q4kxl-tg-16k]], 28.24/44.8 = **0.63×** the 40 GB dense ceiling *(derived)*), then 25.67 / 21.67 / 16.62 at 32k/65k/131k. $8,650/28.24 = **$306.30/measured-tok/s** *(derived)* at 16k. Its dense Qwen3-32B sibling on the same page is **45.72** at 16k (45.72/56.0 = **0.82×** a 32 GB-equivalent read, 1,792/32 *(derived)*), so the 70B file, not the card, is what puts the row under the floor.
> - **SpecFit changes which draft tokens cause a copy. It does not change the ceiling.** [[papers/specfit-offloaded-moe-speculative]] keeps the EAGLE-3 drafter and the target router, and picks the ancestor-closed subtree that maximises expected committed tokens over the per-layer expert union. On Qwen3-30B-A3B with every expert in host RAM, one [[accelerators/nvidia-a100-40gb-pcie]] (rank 33, 77.8 tok/s ceiling), 48 GPU slots per layer: **8.27 tok/s** on CNN/DailyMail against 6.80 for cached EAGLE-3 (**1.22×**, 8.27/6.80 *(derived)*) and 4.67 autoregressive (**1.77×**), and **10.79** on GSM8K against 10.08 (**1.07×**) and 4.70 (**2.30×**). The abstract's 1.97× and 1.72× are against EAGLE-3 with the cache cleared, which the record separates from the cached comparison. On the [[accelerators/nvidia-rtx-4090]] (24 GB, the card the $2,000 sticker prices) with 32 slots the GSM8K gain is **6.70 against 6.05 tok/s** (1.11× *(derived)*). The published runs read routes from an atlas built outside the timer; the record says a deployed selector needs a predictor the paper does not train. 8.27 is an offload measurement on a card whose §4b cell is 136 tok/s resident, so it does not enter that column and it does not move rank 33.

> **Refresh note (docs/fit-matrix-oct14, Oct-18, prior pass).** Re-ran `python tools/fit_matrix_35b.py` on local `main` `4f8ec2e` (236 accelerator records, 218 supply records, 4,415 benchmarks — #268 through #273 on top of the #267 matrix, which was itself stamped on `d17b963`). The selection rule still yields **159 qualifying / 43 priced / 116 unpriced** (69 of the unpriced fit 35B-A3B, 47 do not). Every priced cell was re-diffed against the generator: id, VRAM, bandwidth, the three fit flags, the 35B ceiling, the supply id, the price, the derived $/tok-s, and the order all match the table already below, so **the ranked table does not move** and no rank is renumbered. None of the six commits adds a purchase price. What they add is six Apple bins that miss the bandwidth floor, four more offload-shaped MoE checkpoints (and the active-parameter count the previous note left null), and two more offload papers.
>
> - **Six new Apple records, and none of them clears the gate.** #272 files the #4167 bins the catalog was missing: [[accelerators/local-apple-m1]] (16 GB, **68 GB/s**), [[accelerators/local-apple-m1-pro]] (32 GB, **200 GB/s**), [[accelerators/local-apple-m2]] (24 GB, **100 GB/s**), [[accelerators/local-apple-m2-pro]] (32 GB, **200 GB/s**), [[accelerators/local-apple-m3]] (24 GB, **100 GB/s**) and [[accelerators/local-apple-m3-pro-14core]] (36 GB, **150 GB/s**). The widest of the six is 200/300 = **0.67** of the bandwidth floor *(derived)*, and none has a purchase-price supply record, so the `<= $3,000` arm does not pull them in either. Two of them would fit the 35B-A3B weight bar if the floor were waived: M1 Pro at 32 GB and M3 Pro 14-core at 36 GB are both past the 24 GB line, and M2 Pro at 32 GB is too. Their ceilings, if the floor did not drop them, would be 200/20 = **10.0**, 150/20 = **7.5** and 200/20 = **10.0** tok/s *(derived)* — under the L4's 15.0, which is the slowest row §4 still lists. They do not enter §3 or §4.
> - **The cells on those bins are Llama-2-7B, so they calibrate the bus, not the 20 GB file.** llama.cpp Metal, build `8e672ef`, batch 1, tg128, Q4_0: **14.19** on the M1 7-core and **14.15** on the 8-core ([[benchmarks/llamacpp-metal-m1-7core-llama27b-q4-0-tg128-8e672ef]], [[benchmarks/llamacpp-metal-m1-8core-llama27b-q4-0-tg128-8e672ef]]); **35.52 / 36.41** on the M1 Pro 14-core and 16-core ([[benchmarks/llamacpp-metal-m1-pro-14core-llama27b-q4-0-tg128-8e672ef]], [[benchmarks/llamacpp-metal-m1-pro-16core-llama27b-q4-0-tg128-8e672ef]]); **21.7 / 21.91** on the M2 8-core and 10-core ([[benchmarks/llamacpp-metal-m2-8core-llama27b-q4-0-tg128-8e672ef]], [[benchmarks/llamacpp-metal-m2-10core-llama27b-q4-0-tg128-8e672ef]]); **37.87 / 38.86** on the M2 Pro 16-core and 19-core ([[benchmarks/llamacpp-metal-m2-pro-16core-llama27b-q4-0-tg128-8e672ef]], [[benchmarks/llamacpp-metal-m2-pro-19core-llama27b-q4-0-tg128-8e672ef]]); **21.34** on the M3 10-core ([[benchmarks/llamacpp-metal-m3-10core-llama27b-q4-0-tg128-8e672ef]]); **30.65** on the M3 Pro 14-core ([[benchmarks/llamacpp-metal-m3-pro-14core-llama27b-q4-0-tg128-8e672ef]]). Dividing by the 20 GB denominator — the only ceiling this doc computes, and the wrong byte count for a 7B Q4_0 — gives 14.19/(68/20) = 14.19/3.4 = **4.2×** on the M1 and 36.41/(200/20) = 36.41/10.0 = **3.64×** on the M1 Pro 16-core *(derived)*. Same caveat the RTX 4090 D note already uses: a small-model ratio, not a claim the bin decodes a 35B-A3B at that rate. Not a re-rank.
> - **Four more MoE checkpoints, and the one the previous note left blank now has an active count.** [[models/mimo-v2-6-pro-rl]] was filed with `active_params_b` null; #270 fills it at **42B** from the card's Model Summary (1.02T total, 8 of 384 routed, no shared expert). 42/20 = **2.10×** the 20 GB the 35B column divides by *(derived)* — the dense-equivalent ceiling *overstates* the rate by more than it does for Step 5's 1.35×, and the KV the previous note computed does not change: 10 global layers, **51,200 B/token**, 8,192 tokens = **400 MiB** *(derived)*. [[models/qwen3-5-122b-a10b]] is 121.349B language-model parameters / 10B active, 8 of 256 plus one shared expert. 10/20 = **0.50×** that denominator *(derived)*. Its growing KV is the 12 full-attention layers only: **24,576 B/token**, 24,576/20,480 = **1.20×** the Qwen3.6-35B-A3B geometry *(derived)*, and 8,192 tokens is 24,576 × 8,192 = **192 MiB** *(derived)*. The 36 linear-attention layers are a fixed recurrent state, not part of the per-token number. A 4-bit packing of the language-model count is 121.349e9 / 2 = 60,674,500,000 bytes = **56.51 GiB** *(derived, 4-bit = 0.5 B/param)*, past the 24 GB bar and past the 48 GB the 70B column tests. [[models/qwen3-5-397b-a17b]] is 403.398B / 17B active, 10 of 512 plus one shared expert. 17/20 = **0.85×** *(derived)*. Growing KV is the 15 full-attention layers: **30,720 B/token**, 30,720/20,480 = **1.50×** *(derived)*, and 8,192 tokens is 30,720 × 8,192 = **240 MiB** *(derived)*. The checkpoint is BF16, 403,397,928,944 parameters, so a 4-bit packing is half of that byte count: 403,397,928,944 / 2 = 201,698,964,472 bytes = **187.85 GiB** *(derived)*. [[models/naive-n0-5-flash]] is 309B / 15.5B active, 8 of 256 and no shared expert. 15.5/20 = **0.78×** *(derived)*. There is no full-attention layer: the growing term is the 9 sparse-attention layers, **23,040 B/token**, 23,040/20,480 = **1.12×** *(derived)*, and 8,192 tokens is 23,040 × 8,192 = **180 MiB** *(derived)*. The 39 sliding layers are a fixed 128-token window, **24.4 MiB** *(the record's figure)*. The record's own storage figure is 617,823,283,433 bytes, which is 617,823,283,433 / 1,073,741,824 = **575.2 GiB** *(derived)* and matches the card's BF16 dump. [[models/kimi-k2-6]] is 1,026.879B / 32B active, 8 of 384 plus one shared expert, and the routed experts already ship INT4. 32/20 = **1.60×** *(derived)*. KV is MLA, all 61 layers: **70,272 B/token**, 70,272/70,656 = **0.99×** Step 5's geometry *(derived)*, and 8,192 tokens is 70,272 × 8,192 = **549 MiB** *(derived)*. None of the five has a benchmark, so none enters §4b, and none moves a fit flag: the 24 GB line is a weight test, and every one of these checkpoints is an offload.
> - **HOBBIT is a second copy of each expert, and the card it was timed on is already rank 24.** [[papers/hobbit-mixed-precision-expert-offload]] keeps a high-precision and a low-precision copy of every routed expert and fetches the small one when the gate says the expert is not the top one. The authors' own batch-1 numbers on [[accelerators/nvidia-rtx-4090]] (24 GB, PCIe 4.0, 256 GB host RAM) are **3.21× / 3.29×** decode against MoE-Offloading and **2.30× / 3.92×** against MoE-Infinity, on Mixtral-8x7B and Phi-MoE. The same record says the prefetch by itself is only about **1.05×** once the small copy exists — almost all of the gain is the smaller miss, not the overlap. The 9.93× and the 13.0×/18.9× against llama.cpp are Jetson AGX Orin unified-memory numbers where the baseline is paging off a slow SSD, and the record says so. [[accelerators/nvidia-jetson-agx-orin-64gb]] is the 64 GB bin at 204.8 GB/s, the same shape as the GB10/Thor exclusion already in the note below (under 300 GB/s, no `<= $3,000` price), so it stays outside the 159. A software multiplier on an offload path does not move a ceiling or a rank.
> - **MoE-CORE is the same idea on an Ascend the corpus does not price.** [[papers/moe-core-coordinated-expert-offload]] stages a full expert layer during prefill and a nonuniform per-layer cache during decode, on one Ascend 950PR under an 84 GB cap. Mean TPOT against load-on-demand on DeepSeek-V4-Flash-W4A8 is **1.39× to 1.75×** (38.0 vs 66.5 ms on GSM8K, 40.3 vs 70.6 ms on LongBench v2). Prefetch depth on that model: 42.6 ms with none, **36.0 ms** at one expert (42.6/36.0 = **1.18×**, the record's 15.5%), **35.9 ms** at two and **36.5 ms** at three — the third expert raised TPOT because the copy no longer fit under the remaining compute. There is no Ascend 950PR accelerator record and no purchase price, so it does not enter §3 or §4. The 38–45 ms vs 1,269 ms gap against vLLM prefetch is the paper's own full-layer baseline at a 128-token cap, not a kernel result, and the record says so.

> **Refresh note (docs/fit-matrix-oct14, Oct-18, prior pass).** Re-ran `python tools/fit_matrix_35b.py` on local `main` `d17b963` (230 accelerator records, 218 supply records, 4,382 benchmarks — #263, #264, #265 and #266 on top of the #262 matrix, which was itself stamped on `c104441`). The selection rule still yields **159 qualifying / 43 priced / 116 unpriced** (69 of the unpriced fit 35B-A3B, 47 do not). Every priced cell was re-diffed against the generator: id, VRAM, bandwidth, the three fit flags, the 35B ceiling, the supply id, the price, the derived $/tok-s, and the order all match the table already below, so **the ranked table does not move** and no rank is renumbered. None of the four commits adds an accelerator or a purchase price. What they add is three more offload-shaped MoE checkpoints and one prefetch paper, plus the mid-context Hardware Corner cells the 3090 and 5090 rows in §4b were missing.

> - **Step 5 Preview is a 27B-active model, not the 20 GB file.** [[models/step-5-preview]] is 600B total / 27B active, 8 of 352 experts plus one shared expert of width 1536, and 88 of 92 backbone layers are MoE. The record's own Q4 active footprint is 27e9 × 0.5 = **13.5 GB** of weights read per token *(derived, the record's arithmetic)*, inside the 16 GB the 27B column divides by (13.5 / 16 = **0.84** *(derived)*) and well under the 20 GB the 35B column divides by (27 / 20 = **1.35×** that column's denominator *(derived)* — the active set is larger than the 3B the 35B-A3B column assumes). Its growing KV counts only the 23 full-attention layers: **70,656 B/token**, so 8,192 tokens is 70,656 × 8,192 = 578,813,952 bytes = **552 MiB** *(derived)*. The 69 sliding layers are a fixed 512-token window, **103.5 MiB** *(the record's figure)*, not part of the per-token number. The BF16 checkpoint is about **1,118 GiB** *(derived, 600e9 × 2 / 1,073,741,824)*; the card's serving configs start at FP8 on 4 B300s or 8 H200s. No single-GPU offload recipe and no benchmark, so it does not enter §4b and does not move a fit flag. The 24 GB line is unchanged.

> - **Ling-3.1-flash is the small-active shape again, and MiMo-V2.6-Pro-RL is the one whose KV the 4 GB headroom does not cover.** [[models/ling-3-1-flash]] is 560B total / 25B active, 8 of 512 experts plus one shared expert, FP8 on disk. Its growing KV is the 9 MLA layers only: **10,368 B/token**, 10,368 / 20,480 = **0.51×** the Qwen3.6-35B-A3B geometry *(derived)*, and 8,192 tokens is 10,368 × 8,192 = **81 MiB** *(derived)*. 25 / 20 = **1.25×** the 20 GB denominator *(derived)*. [[models/mimo-v2-6-pro-rl]] is the page's round **1T** with `active_params_b` left null (the card publishes no active count), routing 8 of 384 experts and no shared expert. Its growing KV is the 10 global layers: 10 × 8 × (192 + 128) × 2 = **51,200 B/token**, 51,200 / 20,480 = **2.50×** the 35B-A3B geometry and 51,200 / 70,656 = **0.72×** Step 5's *(derived)*. 8,192 tokens is 51,200 × 8,192 = **400 MiB** *(derived)*, still inside the 4 GB the fit flag reserves; a 128k context is 51,200 × 131,072 = 6,710,886,400 bytes = **6.25 GiB** *(derived)*, which is the headroom and not the weights. The 60 sliding layers are a fixed 128-token window, **39.3 MB** *(the record's figure)*. Neither checkpoint has a benchmark, so neither enters §4b.

> - **DuoServe-MoE is a prefetch schedule, and the cards it was measured on are already in the corpus.** [[papers/duoserve-moe-dual-phase-expert-prefetch]] splits the expert fetch by phase: prefill pipelines one expert at a time, decode predicts the next layer, and the on-device expert cache is sized to k rather than to the pool. The authors' own band is TTFT **1.78× to 5.34×** and end-to-end **1.42× to 7.55×** against on-demand fetch and full-layer prefetch, single request, PCIe 4.0, on an RTX A5000 24 GB and an RTX A6000 48 GB. The A3B-shaped cell is Qwen3-30B-A3B in FP8: TTFT over **5×** versus full-layer prefetch and about **1.95× to 1.99×** versus on-demand fetch, with peak GPU memory **1.54 GB** versus 1.24 / 1.80 / 7.67 GB. [[accelerators/nvidia-rtx-a5000]] is 24 GB at 768 GB/s, a 38.4 tok/s ceiling on the 20 GB column *(derived, 768/20)* and unpriced, so it stays in §4. [[accelerators/nvidia-rtx-a6000]] is already the §4b row at 131.2 tok/s (3.4× its 38.4 floor). The paper's ratios are against other offload schedules, not against that roofline, so they do not enter the × column and do not move a rank.

> - **#264 fills the mid-context cells on the two priced cards §4b already ranks, and the MXFP4 MoE does not fall off the way the Q4_K one does.** Sixty new rows, all llama.cpp llama-bench, batch-1, third-party, on [[accelerators/nvidia-rtx-3090]] (rank 11, $950, 46.8 tok/s ceiling) and [[accelerators/nvidia-rtx-5090]] (rank 12, $1,999, 89.6). The cells that are the workload:
>
>   - **3090, Qwen3.5-35B MXFP4.** **107.1** at 16k, **101.2** at 32k, **93.1** at 64k ([[benchmarks/hc26-3090-qwen35-35b-mxfp4-16k-tg]], [[benchmarks/hc26-3090-qwen35-35b-mxfp4-32k-tg]], [[benchmarks/hc26-3090-qwen35-35b-mxfp4-64k-tg]]). Against the 46.8 floor that is **2.29× / 2.16× / 1.99×** *(derived)*. The Q4_K 30B-A3B series already in §4b is 98.3 / 58.8 / 32.8 at the same three depths, so the MXFP4 cell is 107.1/98.3 = **1.09×** at 16k, 101.2/58.8 = **1.72×** at 32k and 93.1/32.8 = **2.84×** at 64k *(derived)*, and it is still above the floor at 64k where the Q4_K series has already crossed it (0.70×). $950 / 107.1 = **$8.87/measured-tok/s** at 16k and $950 / 93.1 = **$10.20** at 64k *(derived)*. Not a re-rank: a different quant and a different model from the Q4_K series the paragraph below ranks on.
>   - **3090, the dense 27B column, and it is under its own floor.** Qwen3.5-27B Q4_K is **32.3** at 16k and **31.0** at 32k ([[benchmarks/hc26-3090-qwen35-27b-q4k-16k-tg]], [[benchmarks/hc26-3090-qwen35-27b-q4k-32k-tg]]), against the 58.5 tok/s 27B ceiling: 32.3/58.5 = **0.55×** and 31.0/58.5 = **0.53×** *(derived)*. Gemma4-31B Q4_K at 16k is **33.5**, 33.5/58.5 = **0.57×** ([[benchmarks/hc26-3090-gemma4-31b-q4k-16k-tg]]). The MoE/dense ratio on this card at 16k is 107.1/32.3 = **3.32×** *(derived)*. $950 / 32.3 = **$29.41/measured-tok/s** *(derived)*.
>   - **5090, the same MXFP4 file, and the bandwidth ratio does not show up.** **148.3 / 143.2 / 133.5** at 16k / 32k / 64k ([[benchmarks/hc26-5090-qwen35-35b-mxfp4-16k-tg]], [[benchmarks/hc26-5090-qwen35-35b-mxfp4-32k-tg]], [[benchmarks/hc26-5090-qwen35-35b-mxfp4-64k-tg]]), which is 148.3/89.6 = **1.66×**, 143.2/89.6 = **1.60×**, 133.5/89.6 = **1.49×** *(derived)* — still above the floor at 64k, and a smaller margin than the 3090's because the 5090's floor is higher. 148.3/107.1 = **1.38×**, 143.2/101.2 = **1.42×**, 133.5/93.1 = **1.43×** *(derived)*, against a bandwidth ratio of 1,792/936 = **1.91×**. $1,999 / 148.3 = **$13.48/measured-tok/s** at 16k *(derived)*, which does not beat the 3090's $8.87 on the same file.
>   - **5090, dense, also under its own floor.** Qwen3.5-27B Q4_K is **55.8 / 53.8 / 50.1** at 16k / 32k / 64k ([[benchmarks/hc26-5090-qwen35-27b-q4k-16k-tg]], [[benchmarks/hc26-5090-qwen35-27b-q4k-32k-tg]], [[benchmarks/hc26-5090-qwen35-27b-q4k-64k-tg]]): 55.8/112.0 = **0.50×**, 53.8/112.0 = **0.48×**, 50.1/112.0 = **0.45×** *(derived)*. Gemma4-31B Q4_K is **59.2 / 55.4 / 51.6** (0.53× / 0.49× / 0.46×, [[benchmarks/hc26-5090-gemma4-31b-q4k-16k-tg]] and the 32k and 64k siblings) and Qwen3-32B Q4_K at 16k is **50.9** (0.45×, [[benchmarks/hc26-5090-qwen3-32b-q4k-16k-tg]]). The MoE/dense ratio at 16k is 148.3/55.8 = **2.66×** *(derived)*. $1,999 / 55.8 = **$35.82/measured-tok/s** *(derived)*.
>   - **The smaller files stay above the 20 GB floor for longer, and they are not the workload.** Gemma4-26B Q4_K on the 3090 is **115.0 / 107.5 / 98.9** at 16k / 32k / 64k (2.46× / 2.30× / 2.11× the 46.8 floor, [[benchmarks/hc26-3090-gemma4-26b-q4k-16k-tg]] and siblings); on the 5090 it is **167.2 / 159.4 / 149.4** (1.87× / 1.78× / 1.67×, [[benchmarks/hc26-5090-gemma4-26b-q4k-16k-tg]] and siblings). gpt-oss-20B Q4_K on the 5090 is **249.2 / 215.1 / 169.0** (2.78× / 2.40× / 1.89×, [[benchmarks/hc26-5090-gptoss-20b-q4k-16k-tg]] and siblings). Qwen3-14B Q4_K on the 5090 is the one that does cross: **123.8** at 4k (1.38×), **102.7** at 16k (1.15×), **82.4** at 32k (0.92×), **57.6** at 64k (0.64×), **37.2** at 128k (0.42×) — [[benchmarks/hc26-5090-qwen3-14b-q4k-4k-tg]] through [[benchmarks/hc26-5090-qwen3-14b-q4k-128k-tg]], all ÷ 89.6 *(derived)*. A 14B dense file crossing the 20 GB floor is KV, not weights. The prefill siblings are not a decode ceiling and are not cited as one.

> **Refresh note (docs/fit-matrix-oct14, Oct-18, prior pass).** Re-ran `python tools/fit_matrix_35b.py` on local `main` `c104441` (230 accelerator records, 218 supply records, 4,306 benchmarks — #257, #258 and #259 on top of the #256 matrix, which was itself stamped on `e525b48`). The selection rule still yields **159 qualifying / 43 priced / 116 unpriced** (69 of the unpriced fit 35B-A3B, 47 do not). Every priced cell was re-diffed against the generator: id, VRAM, bandwidth, the three fit flags, the 35B ceiling, the supply id, the price, the derived $/tok-s, and the order all match the table already below, so **the ranked table does not move** and no rank is renumbered. None of the three commits adds an accelerator or a purchase price. What they add is the named 35B-A3B checkpoint the 20 GB column was written for, plus two larger MoE checkpoints and one prefetch paper.

> - **The 20 GB column now has the model the brief names.** [[models/qwen3-6-35b-a3b]] is 35.952B total / 3B active, 256 experts with 8 routed plus one shared expert, and its KV is **20,480 B/token** — the same geometry the previous note recorded for [[models/ornith-1-5-35b-a3b]]. The record's own safetensors count is 35,951,822,704 BF16 parameters, so the checkpoint is 35,951,822,704 × 2 = 71,903,645,408 bytes = 71,903,645,408 / 1,073,741,824 = **66.97 GiB** *(derived)*. A 4-bit packing of the same count is a quarter of that, 66.97 / 4 = **16.74 GiB** *(derived)*, inside the 20 GB the ceiling divides by. The 4 GB headroom at 8,192 tokens is 20,480 × 8,192 = 167,772,160 bytes = **160 MiB** *(derived)*, so the 4 GB the fit flag reserves is runtime and the OS, not the KV. Same threshold as before: nothing in the new record moves the 24 GB line, and there is no benchmark of it, so it does not enter §4b.

> - **Two more MoE checkpoints, and neither is the 20 GB file.** [[models/aliceai-foundation-80b-a3b-base]] activates the same 3B per token (10 of 512 experts plus one shared expert) but the record's safetensors count is 81,286,433,408 BF16 parameters, so the checkpoint is 81,286,433,408 × 2 = 162,572,866,816 bytes = **151.41 GiB** *(derived)* and a 4-bit packing is 151.41 / 4 = **37.85 GiB** *(derived)*. That clears the 35B-A3B 24 GB bar and misses the 70B-Q4 48 GB bar, and its KV is 24,576 B/token, so 8,192 tokens is 24,576 × 8,192 = **192 MiB** *(derived)*. [[models/beam-1-0-501b-a23b]] is 501.3B total / 23.4B active, 8 of 256 experts and no shared expert. The record's BF16 count is 501,203,374,528 parameters, so a 4-bit packing is 501,203,374,528 / 2 = 250,601,687,264 bytes = **233.39 GiB** *(derived)* — past every priced row except the 512 GB [[accelerators/apple-m3-ultra]], and past the 48 GB the 70B column tests. Its growing KV is only the 9 full-attention layers, **36,864 B/token**, which at 8,192 tokens is 36,864 × 8,192 = **288 MiB** *(derived)*; the record's own note puts the 43 sliding layers at a fixed **172.5 MiB** that does not grow with context. The active-byte read is the other way around: 23.4 / 20 = **1.17×** *(derived)* the 20 GB the ceiling divides by, so for this checkpoint the dense-equivalent ceiling *overstates* the rate, where for the 3B-active models it understates it.

> - **[[papers/promoe-proactive-expert-prefetch]] is why a card the fit flag marks `-` can still serve the MoE, and it does not change a rank.** With routed experts in host RAM the miss is only known when the expert GEMM is about to run, so the PCIe fetch sits on the critical path; the paper's fix is to predict the next layer's experts from the current hidden state and copy them while the current layer computes, then preempt with the real gate result. Every number in the record is the authors' own, on one [[accelerators/nvidia-rtx-4090]] (rank 24, already `Y/Y/-` at 24 GB) at batch 1 over PCIe 4.0: decode **2.07×** average and **5.02×** best against transformers with an LRU expert cache, and only **1.34×** average against llama.cpp, because a faster GPU-side MoE kernel leaves less idle compute to hide the copy behind. The same record says the gain falls further as the batch grows (batch 4 cuts the decode speedup from 2.28× to 1.59×). That is a software multiplier on an offload path, not a bandwidth or a price, so it does not move a ceiling, a fit flag, or the $/tok-s order. Ranks 1 and 2 stay `-/-/-`.

> **Refresh note (docs/fit-matrix-oct14, Oct-18, prior pass).** Re-ran `python tools/fit_matrix_35b.py` on local `main` `e525b48` (230 accelerator records, 218 supply records, 4,322 benchmarks — #249 through #255 on top of the #248 matrix, which was itself stamped on `d72800f`). The selection rule still yields **159 qualifying / 43 priced / 116 unpriced** (69 of the unpriced fit 35B-A3B, 47 do not). Every priced cell was re-diffed against the generator: id, VRAM, bandwidth, the three fit flags, the 35B ceiling, the supply id, the price, the derived $/tok-s, and the order all match the table already below, so **the ranked table does not move** and no rank is renumbered. None of the seven commits adds an accelerator or a purchase price. What they add is model geometry, and one technique record that explains why a card under 24 GB can still serve the MoE the fit flag marks `-`.

> - **The 20 GB column is now the named checkpoint, not only the brief.** [[models/ornith-1-5-35b-a3b]] is 35.952B total / ~3B active, `qwen3_5_moe`, 256 experts with 8 routed, and its KV is **20,480 B/token** — the same geometry as [[models/qwen3-5-35b-a3b]] (35B / 3B, 20,480 B/token). BF16 of that checkpoint is 71,903,645,408 bytes, which is 71,903,645,408 / 1,073,741,824 = **66.97 GiB** *(derived)*. A 4-bit packing of the same count is about a quarter of that, 66.97 / 4 = **16.74 GiB** *(derived)*, inside the 20 GB the ceiling divides by. The 4 GB headroom at 8,192 tokens is 20,480 × 8,192 = 167,772,160 bytes = **160 MiB** *(derived)*, so the 4 GB the fit flag reserves is runtime and the OS, not the KV. Nothing in the new record changes the 24 GB threshold, and there is no benchmark of it, so it does not enter §4b.

> - **Three more records sit in the same ~3–4B-active class and none of them is the 20 GB file.** [[models/kolibri-1]] is 78.103B / 3.458B active, KV 20,480 B/token, but 78.103 × 2 = **156.2 GB** of BF16 *(derived, one-pass 2 bytes/param)* — a resident Q4 of that is closer to 40 GB than to 20 GB, so it is a 70B-column problem, not a 35B one. [[models/xing4-0-29b-a4b]] is 31.215B / 4B active with KV **46,080 B/token**, more than double the 20,480 of the named MoE, so its context headroom is the thing that moves, not the weight bar. [[models/mellum2-1-12b-a2-5b-thinking]] is 12.15B / 2.5B active and the record's own note puts the BF16 checkpoint at about 24 GB; a Q4 of it is a small-model file and does not use the 20 GB denominator.

> - **The dense 27B sibling and the two Flash-class records do not move a byte assumption either.** [[models/qwen3-8-27b]] is dense, 27.781B, KV 65,536 B/token: 27.781 × 0.5 = **13.89 GB** at 4-bit *(derived)*, inside the 16 GB the 27B column divides by. [[models/mimo-v2-6-flash-rl]] is 309B / 15B active (KV 23,040 B/token) and [[models/glm-5-3-flash]] is 320B / 18B active (KV 11,264 B/token, geometry filled by #251). Both are expert-offload machines, not single-device 24 GB fits, so neither gets a fit flag here. [[models/ornith-1-5-9b]] is a 9.653B dense model and is out of scope.

> - **#255 is why a `-` on 35B is not the same as "cannot run it," and it does not change a rank.** [[papers/moe-infinity-sparsity-aware-expert-cache]] and [[flops/moe-infinity-expert-cache-prefetch]] record a host-RAM expert cache: attention, shared experts and the KV stay resident, and a request-scoped activation trace picks which routed expert to DMA over PCIe. The authors' own range is **3.1–16.7×** against other offload systems (abstract and Table 1) and **2.7–13.7×** in the conclusion, with PCIe 4.0 stated as both 32 GB/s (Appendix B.2) and 24 GB/s (Table 1 caption) — vendor-paper figures, not a measurement in this corpus, and explicitly not a speedup against a fully resident GPU. A Mixtral-shaped MoE only gains 1.4×. That is the mechanism behind the sentence in §2 that the 20 GB ceiling is a conservative floor for MoE: the bytes moved per token can be the active experts, not the resident file. It does not add a tok/s, a price, or a `Y`, so [[accelerators/nvidia-tesla-p100]] and [[accelerators/intel-arc-b580]] stay `-/-/-` at ranks 1 and 2.

> - **#252 is a kernel constraint on two priced rows, not a new ceiling.** [[flops/tt-metal-flash-decode-kv-split]] says the TT-Metal decode SDPA splits KV across Tensix cores and only runs multi-query attention on a single token. [[accelerators/tenstorrent-wormhole-n300]] (rank 27, 24 GB, 576 GB/s, **Y/Y/-**, ceiling 576/20 = **28.8** tok/s *(derived)*, $1,449, **$50.31/tok-s** *(derived)*) and [[accelerators/tenstorrent-wormhole-n150]] (rank 33, 12 GB, `-/-/-`) are the parts that constraint lands on. Most models since Llama-2 are grouped-query, including the named 35B-A3B (2 KV heads, 16 query heads). The fit flag is unchanged because it is a byte test; the fast decode path does not exist yet for that attention shape.


> **Refresh note (docs/fit-matrix-oct14, Oct-17, this pass).** Re-ran `python tools/fit_matrix_35b.py` on local `main` `d72800f` (230 accelerator records, 218 supply records, 4,305 benchmarks — #246 on top of `841d639`). The selection rule still yields **159 qualifying / 43 priced / 116 unpriced** (69 of the unpriced fit 35B-A3B, 47 do not). Every priced cell was re-diffed against the generator: id, VRAM, bandwidth, the three fit flags, the 35B ceiling, the supply id, the price, the derived $/tok-s, and the order all match, so **the ranked table does not move** and no rank below is renumbered. #246 adds no accelerator and no purchase price. What it adds is the first Qwen3.5-35B-A3B and Qwen3.5-27B llama.cpp cells for the W7900 and a second protocol for the R9700, plus an MLX long-decode scorecard on the rank-41 Mac Studio.

> - **R9700 stays unpriced, and a second Q4 of the named MoE clears the same floor the earlier cell did.** [[accelerators/amd-radeon-ai-pro-r9700]] stays 32 GB / 640 GB/s, **Y/Y/-** (§4). Ceilings: 640/20 = **32.0**, 640/16 = **40.0**, 640/40 = **16.0** tok/s *(derived)*. llama.cpp, batch 1, one request, ~2.9K prompt, unsloth UD-Q4_K_M (21 GB on disk), decodes at **133.0** on AMDVLK, **112.0** on RADV and **68.8** on ROCm ([[benchmarks/nm-1s4bggo-r9700-35b-amdvlk-q4km-2k9-tg]], [[benchmarks/nm-1s4bggo-r9700-35b-radv-q4km-2k9-tg]], [[benchmarks/nm-1s4bggo-r9700-35b-rocm-q4km-2k9-tg]]). 133.0/32.0 = **4.16×**, 112.0/32.0 = **3.50×**, 68.8/32.0 = **2.15×** *(derived)*. Prefill on the same three backends is **1,030**, **2,987** and **1,190** ([[benchmarks/nm-1s4bggo-r9700-35b-amdvlk-q4km-2k9-pp]], [[benchmarks/nm-1s4bggo-r9700-35b-radv-q4km-2k9-pp]], [[benchmarks/nm-1s4bggo-r9700-35b-rocm-q4km-2k9-pp]]); 2987/1030 = **2.90×** and 2987/1190 = **2.51×** *(derived)* — RADV leads prefill and AMDVLK leads decode, so the three are not averaged. Set against the §4b row already on file, 133.0/114.19 = **1.16×** the Vulkan server-endpoint cell and 112.0/114.19 = **0.981×** it *(derived)*; 68.8/76.43 = **0.90×** the ROCm 10.0 sibling *(derived)*. Different harness, different Q4 file (Q4_K_M vs UD-Q4_K_XL), same card, and the RADV pair agrees to about 2%. The new AMDVLK cell is the faster of the two and is the one §4b now quotes; the 114.19 row stays. The dense sibling on the same machine is under its own floor again: Qwen3.5-27B Q4_K_M (16 GB) decodes at **30.6** / **30.4** / **25.2** ([[benchmarks/nm-1s4bggo-r9700-27b-amdvlk-q4km-2k9-tg]], [[benchmarks/nm-1s4bggo-r9700-27b-radv-q4km-2k9-tg]], [[benchmarks/nm-1s4bggo-r9700-27b-rocm-q4km-2k9-tg]]), 30.6/40.0 = **0.77×**, 30.4/40.0 = **0.76×**, 25.2/40.0 = **0.63×** *(derived)*. 133.0/30.6 = **4.35×** *(derived)* on one card, two models, and again the MoE is the one above the floor. No street price, so §3 does not move.

> - **W7900 fits all three workloads and was missing a measured cell; it now has one, and it is still unpriced.** [[accelerators/amd-radeon-pro-w7900]] stays 48 GB / 864 GB/s, **Y/Y/Y** (§4). Ceilings: 864/20 = **43.2**, 864/16 = **54.0**, 864/40 = **21.6** tok/s *(derived)*. The same llama.cpp protocol decodes the 21 GB Q4_K_M at **123.7** AMDVLK, **114.3** RADV and **78.9** ROCm ([[benchmarks/nm-1s4bggo-w7900-35b-amdvlk-q4km-2k9-tg]], [[benchmarks/nm-1s4bggo-w7900-35b-radv-q4km-2k9-tg]], [[benchmarks/nm-1s4bggo-w7900-35b-rocm-q4km-2k9-tg]]). 123.7/43.2 = **2.86×**, 114.3/43.2 = **2.65×**, 78.9/43.2 = **1.83×** *(derived)*. Prefill is **948** / **2,326** / **1,001** ([[benchmarks/nm-1s4bggo-w7900-35b-amdvlk-q4km-2k9-pp]], [[benchmarks/nm-1s4bggo-w7900-35b-radv-q4km-2k9-pp]], [[benchmarks/nm-1s4bggo-w7900-35b-rocm-q4km-2k9-pp]]); 2326/948 = **2.45×** and 2326/1001 = **2.32×** *(derived)* — same backend split as the R9700, not averaged. The dense 27B is under its floor on every backend: **31.8** / **31.8** / **24.4** ([[benchmarks/nm-1s4bggo-w7900-27b-amdvlk-q4km-2k9-tg]], [[benchmarks/nm-1s4bggo-w7900-27b-radv-q4km-2k9-tg]], [[benchmarks/nm-1s4bggo-w7900-27b-rocm-q4km-2k9-tg]]), 31.8/54.0 = **0.59×** and 24.4/54.0 = **0.45×** *(derived)*. 123.7/31.8 = **3.89×** *(derived)*. AMDVLK on the 48 GB card versus AMDVLK on the 32 GB card is 123.7/133.0 = **0.93×** *(derived)* — the wider bus (864/640 = **1.35×**, *(derived)*) does not show up in the MoE decode, which is what the ~3B-active caveat in §2 predicts. No purchase price, so no $/tok-s and nothing enters §3. The AMDVLK cell is now in §4b.

> - **The two-card sweep is a combined rate, and it is slower than either card alone.** W7900 + R9700, tensor-split, AMDVLK, same 21 GB file, decodes at **82.1** at 8,824 tokens of context and **34.3** at 215,917 ([[benchmarks/nm-1s4bggo-w7900-r9700-35b-amdvlk-ctx8824-tg]], [[benchmarks/nm-1s4bggo-w7900-r9700-35b-amdvlk-ctx215917-tg]]). The record says the figure covers both cards and the split ratio is unpublished, so it is not divided by 2 and it is not a per-card rate. Adding the two ceilings is 32.0+43.2 = **75.2** tok/s *(derived)*; 82.1/75.2 = **1.09×** and 34.3/75.2 = **0.46×** *(derived)*. The ROCm sibling at the short depth is **74.8** ([[benchmarks/nm-1s4bggo-w7900-r9700-35b-rocm-ctx8824-tg]]), 74.8/75.2 = **0.99×** *(derived)*. 82.1/133.0 = **0.62×** the single R9700 and 82.1/123.7 = **0.66×** the single W7900 *(derived)* — splitting this MoE across the two cards costs decode at every depth the sweep prints. The short cell is inside 10% of a summed floor and the long one is under it; neither replaces the single-card §4b rows, and neither is a price.

> - **M3 Ultra stays rank 41, and the new long-decode cell is the named MoE on a 256 GB machine the $9,000 sticker is not.** [[accelerators/apple-m3-ultra]] stays 512 GB / 819 GB/s, **Y/Y/Y**, $9,000 ([[supply/used-mac-studio-m3-ultra-512gb]]), **$219.78/(tok/s)** *(derived)*. The record's bandwidth is 819 GB/s for both the 60-core and the 80-core SKU, so the ceiling does not change with the core count: 819/20 = **40.95**, rounded to the **41.0** the table prints; 819/16 = **51.2**, 819/40 = **20.5** *(derived)*. rapid-mlx on a **256 GB** M3 Ultra decodes Qwen3.5-35B-A3B 4-bit at **95.2** tok/s long and Qwen3.5-27B 4-bit at **37.7** ([[benchmarks/rapidmlx-m3ultra256-qwen35-35b-a3b-long-decode]], [[benchmarks/rapidmlx-m3ultra256-qwen35-27b-long-decode]]); cold TTFT is **322 ms** and **453 ms** ([[benchmarks/rapidmlx-m3ultra256-qwen35-35b-a3b-cold-ttft]], [[benchmarks/rapidmlx-m3ultra256-qwen35-27b-cold-ttft]]). 95.2/41.0 = **2.32×** the 35B floor and 37.7/51.2 = **0.74×** the 27B floor *(derived)*. 95.2/37.7 = **2.52×** *(derived)* — same machine, and again the MoE is the one above its floor. The 8-bit sibling of the same MoE is **80.0** ([[benchmarks/rapidmlx-m3ultra256-qwen35-35b-a3b-8bit-long-decode]]), 80.0/41.0 = **1.95×** and 80.0/95.2 = **0.84×** *(derived)*. The token count behind "long" is unpublished, so this is not a tg128 cell and it is not averaged with anything. The scorecard machine is 256 GB; the rank-41 price is the 512 GB Studio. Pairing $9,000 with 95.2 tok/s would be the cross-capacity join §6 already refuses, so **$9,000/95.2 = $94.54 is not a re-rank** *(derived, and not used)*. The 256 GB row at $6,250 is the one the capacity matches ([[supply/used-mac-studio-m3-ultra-256gb]]), and it stays unjoined for the reason already in §6. Qwen3-Coder-Next 4-bit, the Next-class file, decodes at **73.5** ([[benchmarks/rapidmlx-m3ultra256-qwen3-coder-next-long-decode]]), 73.5/41.0 = **1.79×** *(derived)*; the 6-bit sibling is **65.6**, 65.6/41.0 = **1.60×** and 65.6/73.5 = **0.89×** *(derived)* ([[benchmarks/rapidmlx-m3ultra256-qwen3-coder-next-6bit-long-decode]]). GLM-4.7-Flash 8-bit is **57.2**, 57.2/41.0 = **1.40×** *(derived)* ([[benchmarks/rapidmlx-m3ultra256-glm47-flash-long-decode]]). None of those is the 20 GB denominator, so only the 4-bit 35B-A3B cell enters §4b, and the rank does not move.

> **Refresh note (docs/fit-matrix-oct14, Oct-17, this pass).** Re-ran `python tools/fit_matrix_35b.py` on local `main` `267e65c` (230 accelerator records, 218 supply records, 4,203 benchmarks — #242 and #243, the same Xeon 6 MLPerf v6.1 ingest, on top of `3c5cf8e`). The selection rule still yields **159 qualifying / 43 priced / 116 unpriced** (69 of the unpriced fit 35B-A3B, 47 do not). Every priced cell was re-diffed against the generator: id, VRAM, bandwidth, the three fit flags, the 35B ceiling, the supply id, the price, the derived $/tok-s, and the order all match, so **the ranked table does not move** and no rank below is renumbered. No new supply record, so no new price. What landed is four Granite Rapids host CPUs plus sixteen llama3.1-8b INT8 aggregate rows and one whisper samples/s row. None of the four enters the 159.
>
> - **The four Xeon 6 records are the same shape as the unpriced Genoa siblings, and they stay out for the same reason.** [[accelerators/intel-xeon-6787p-granite-rapids]] and [[accelerators/intel-xeon-6788p-granite-rapids]] are 86-core, 8-channel, **409.6 GB/s** *(record)*. [[accelerators/intel-xeon-6972p-granite-rapids]] (96-core) and [[accelerators/intel-xeon-6980p-granite-rapids]] (128-core) are 12-channel, **614.4 GB/s** *(record)*. All four set `unified_memory: true` and leave `vram_gb` null — capacity is how many RDIMMs are fitted, and ARK's max is 3 TB, which is not a configured pool. `effective_vram` returns None unless the record has a `vram_gb` or a `UNIFIED_MEM_GB` override, and the only overrides in the tool are the Strix Halo 128 GB pool and the Genoa 384 GB self-build. There is no Xeon supply record to read a configured capacity off, and no purchase price at `<= $3,000`, so the 8 GB gate drops them before the bandwidth floor is even tested. The same drop already applies to [[accelerators/amd-epyc-9454p-genoa]] (460.8 GB/s) and [[accelerators/amd-epyc-9334-genoa-dual]] (921.6 GB/s): bandwidth alone does not qualify a host CPU whose pool is unpublished. Inventing a 512 GB or 1 TB fit flag so the 614.4 GB/s parts could show `Y/Y/Y` would pair a ceiling with a machine the corpus does not price. Not done.
> - **The bandwidth arithmetic, stated and not ranked.** If a configured pool large enough for the 24 GB bar were on file, the 35B ceilings would be 409.6/20 = **20.48** tok/s on the 8-channel parts and 614.4/20 = **30.72** tok/s on the 12-channel parts *(derived)*. The 27B column would be 409.6/16 = **25.6** and 614.4/16 = **38.4** *(derived)*; the 70B column 409.6/40 = **10.24** and 614.4/40 = **15.36** *(derived)*. Set against the one host CPU this doc does rank, [[accelerators/amd-epyc-9554-genoa]] at 460.8 GB/s and a 23.0 tok/s ceiling, the 12-channel Xeon bus is 614.4/460.8 = **1.33×** that floor *(derived)* and the 8-channel bus is 409.6/460.8 = **0.89×** *(derived)*. None of those ceilings is a row: there is no pool and no sticker, so there is no $/tok-s and nothing enters §3.
> - **The new cells are node-level INT8 aggregates of an 8B, not a 35B-A3B decode.** Intel's own 2S 6980P box does llama3.1-8b INT8 at **1,916.74** tok/s Offline and **1,139.51** Server ([[benchmarks/mlperf-v6-1-intel-2x6980p-llama31-8b-int8-offline]], [[benchmarks/mlperf-v6-1-intel-2x6980p-llama31-8b-int8-server]]). Supermicro's 2S 6980P is **1,833.97** / **1,101.19** ([[benchmarks/mlperf-v6-1-smci-2x6980p-llama31-8b-int8-offline]], [[benchmarks/mlperf-v6-1-smci-2x6980p-llama31-8b-int8-server]]). 1833.97/1916.74 = **0.957×** *(derived)* — about 4% apart, two submitters, not averaged. Red Hat's 2S 6972P is **1,507.21** / **644.788** ([[benchmarks/mlperf-v6-1-redhat-2x6972p-llama31-8b-int8-offline]], [[benchmarks/mlperf-v6-1-redhat-2x6972p-llama31-8b-int8-server]]); 1507.21/1916.74 = **0.786×** *(derived)* the 128-core box at the same 12-channel width. Dell's 2S 6787P (vLLM, FP8 KV, 16×64 GB DDR5-6400) is **1,227.99** / **516.012** ([[benchmarks/mlperf-v6-1-dell-2x6787p-llama31-8b-int8-offline]], [[benchmarks/mlperf-v6-1-dell-2x6787p-llama31-8b-int8-server]]); Supermicro's 2S 6787P is **1,189.89** ([[benchmarks/mlperf-v6-1-smci-2x6787p-llama31-8b-int8-offline]]), 1189.89/1227.99 = **0.969×** *(derived)*. Quanta's 2S 6788P is **1,170.51** / **516.012** ([[benchmarks/mlperf-v6-1-qct-2x6788p-llama31-8b-int8-offline]], [[benchmarks/mlperf-v6-1-qct-2x6788p-llama31-8b-int8-server]]). The 4-socket 6788P rows do not double the 2-socket ones: Cisco Offline **2,068.63** and Supermicro Offline **2,063.24** ([[benchmarks/mlperf-v6-1-cisco-4x6788p-llama31-8b-int8-offline]], [[benchmarks/mlperf-v6-1-smci-4x6788p-llama31-8b-int8-offline]]), 2068.63/1170.51 = **1.77×** *(derived)* and 2063.24/2068.63 = **0.997×** *(derived)*. Server on those 4S boxes is **1,030.45** and **966.241** ([[benchmarks/mlperf-v6-1-cisco-4x6788p-llama31-8b-int8-server]], [[benchmarks/mlperf-v6-1-smci-4x6788p-llama31-8b-int8-server]]). Dividing any of these by the 20 GB ceiling is the wrong comparison twice over: the metric is `tps_aggregate` across 2 or 4 sockets, and the model is llama3.1-8b INT8, not a Q4 35B-A3B. They do not enter §4b.
> - **The whisper row is not tokens.** Dell's 2S 6980P whisper INT8 Offline is **2,275.93 samples/s** ([[benchmarks/mlperf-v6-1-dell-2x6980p-whisper-int8-offline]]), stored under `tps_aggregate` because that is the aggregate-throughput member of the metric enum. The unit field says samples/s. It is not divided into a tok/s ceiling.

> **Refresh note (docs/fit-matrix-oct14, Oct-17, prior pass).** Re-ran `python tools/fit_matrix_35b.py` on local `main` `3c5cf8e` (226 accelerator records, 218 supply records, 4,187 benchmarks — #240 on top of `80aee58`). The selection rule still yields **159 qualifying / 43 priced / 116 unpriced** (69 of the unpriced fit 35B-A3B, 47 do not). Every priced cell was re-diffed against the generator: id, VRAM, bandwidth, the three fit flags, the 35B ceiling, the supply id, the price, the derived $/tok-s, and the order all match, so **the ranked table does not move** and no rank below is renumbered. #240 adds no accelerator and no purchase price. What it adds is the Next/Flash class itself, at Q4 and with MTP, on the rank-37 box, plus two larger Flash MoEs at Q2 and a 1.2B calibration on a card §4 already lists.
>
> - **Strix Halo 8060S stays rank 37, and the named Next/Flash Q4 sits just above the 20 GB floor.** [[accelerators/amd-radeon-8060s-ryzen-ai-max]] stays 128 GB unified / 256 GB/s, **Y/Y/Y**, $1,900 → $148.44/tok-s *(derived)* ([[supply/new-minisforum-ms-s1-max-128gb]]). The 35B ceiling is 256/20 = **12.8** tok/s *(derived)*. [[models/qwen3-8-flash-next]] is 180B total / 6B active, not the 35B-A3B and not the 27B. DwarfStar on ROCm 10.0, speculative decoding off, decodes the Q4 at **19.5233** tok/s at depth 0 and **19.3232** at depth 65536 ([[benchmarks/llmb-8060s-dwarfstar-rocm10-qwen38-flash-next-q4-d0-decode]], [[benchmarks/llmb-8060s-dwarfstar-rocm10-qwen38-flash-next-q4-d64k-decode]]); prefill at depth 0 is **504.5011** ([[benchmarks/llmb-8060s-dwarfstar-rocm10-qwen38-flash-next-q4-d0-prefill]]). 19.5233/12.8 = **1.53×** and 19.3232/12.8 = **1.51×** the 35B floor *(derived)*. 19.3232/19.5233 = **0.990×** *(derived)* — filling 64k of KV costs about a percent of the decode. The llama.cpp ROCm 10.0 sibling on UD-IQ4_XS is **20.5757** at depth 0 and **12.2638** at depth 65536 ([[benchmarks/llmb-8060s-rocm10-qwen38-flash-next-iq4xs-d0-decode]], [[benchmarks/llmb-8060s-rocm10-qwen38-flash-next-iq4xs-d64k-decode]]); prefill **470.2468** ([[benchmarks/llmb-8060s-rocm10-qwen38-flash-next-iq4xs-d0-prefill]]). 20.5757/12.8 = **1.61×** and 12.2638/12.8 = **0.96×** *(derived)*. 20.5757/19.5233 = **1.05×** at depth 0 *(derived)*, and 12.2638/20.5757 = **0.596×** *(derived)* — the same file class loses two fifths of its decode by 64k on llama.cpp and essentially none on DwarfStar. Different engine, different quant label, so the two depth-0 numbers are not averaged. $1,900/19.5233 = **$97.32 per measured tok/s** *(derived)* and $1,900/20.5757 = **$92.34** *(derived)*. Both price a measured Q4 of a 180B/6B model, not the 12.8 ceiling the $148.44 rank uses, so the rank does not move. Neither cell is the 35B-A3B §4b ranks, so neither enters that table.
> - **MTP on the same model roughly doubles the decode, and it is still not the floor.** The retained-PM4 llama-server build (shared MTP head, n-gram draft, draft-n-max 3) decodes Qwen3.8-Flash-Next at **43.9** tok/s and prefills pp2048 at **1,207.0** ([[benchmarks/kyuz0-8060s-strixllama-qwen38-flash-next-mtp-decode]], [[benchmarks/kyuz0-8060s-strixllama-qwen38-flash-next-mtp-pp2048]]). 43.9/12.8 = **3.43×** the 35B floor *(derived)*. 43.9/19.5233 = **2.25×** the DwarfStar Q4 baseline and 43.9/20.5757 = **2.13×** the llama.cpp IQ4_XS cell *(derived)* — the record's own note calls it roughly double the 19.5–20.6 tok/s no-speculation baseline, and the arithmetic agrees. Speculation is a second draft model, not a bandwidth change, so 43.9 does not replace either baseline and does not enter §4b. $1,900/43.9 = **$43.28 per measured tok/s** *(derived)*.
> - **The two larger Flash MoEs are the wrong byte count, and one of them is not fully resident.** DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731 UD-IQ2_XXS ([[models/deepseek-v4-flash]], 284B / 13B active) decodes at **13.3312** at depth 0 and **10.4007** at depth 65536 ([[benchmarks/llmb-8060s-rocm10-dsv4-flash-iq2xxs-d0-decode]], [[benchmarks/llmb-8060s-rocm10-dsv4-flash-iq2xxs-d64k-decode]]); prefill **216.9735** ([[benchmarks/llmb-8060s-rocm10-dsv4-flash-iq2xxs-d0-prefill]]). 13.3312/12.8 = **1.04×** and 10.4007/12.8 = **0.81×** *(derived)*. 10.4007/13.3312 = **0.78×** *(derived)*. IQ2 is not the Q4 the 20 GB denominator assumes. DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash Q2 ([[models/deepseek-v4-1-flash-1017]], 552B backbone, 8B active at prefill) on DwarfStar decodes at **14.7622** and **14.3066** ([[benchmarks/llmb-8060s-dwarfstar-rocm10-dsv41-flash-q2-d0-decode]], [[benchmarks/llmb-8060s-dwarfstar-rocm10-dsv41-flash-q2-d64k-decode]]); prefill **240.8634** ([[benchmarks/llmb-8060s-dwarfstar-rocm10-dsv41-flash-q2-d0-prefill]]). 14.7622/12.8 = **1.15×** and 14.3066/12.8 = **1.12×** *(derived)*. 14.3066/14.7622 = **0.969×** *(derived)*. The prefill record says the Q2 main weights are 151.77 GiB plus 188.83 GiB of Engram tables streamed from disk, so this is not a resident run and the `Y` on the 128 GB pool does not cover it. GLM-5.3-Flash Q2 ([[models/glm-5-3-flash]], 320B / 18B active) decodes at **11.9241** and **9.159** ([[benchmarks/llmb-8060s-dwarfstar-rocm10-glm53-flash-q2-d0-decode]], [[benchmarks/llmb-8060s-dwarfstar-rocm10-glm53-flash-q2-d64k-decode]]); prefill **227.1627** ([[benchmarks/llmb-8060s-dwarfstar-rocm10-glm53-flash-q2-d0-prefill]]). 11.9241/12.8 = **0.93×** and 9.159/12.8 = **0.72×** *(derived)*. 9.159/11.9241 = **0.77×** *(derived)*. A Q2 of a 320B file is under the dense-equivalent floor on this bus. None of the three enters §4b, and none has a street price of its own, so §3 does not move. $1,900/13.3312 = **$142.52**, $1,900/14.7622 = **$128.71**, $1,900/11.9241 = **$159.34** per measured tok/s *(derived)* — all three price the wrong byte count.
> - **R9700, still unpriced, and the new cell is a 1.2B, not the 35B-A3B.** [[accelerators/amd-radeon-ai-pro-r9700]] stays 32 GB / 640 GB/s, **Y/Y/-** (§4). Ceiling 640/20 = **32.0** tok/s *(derived)*. LFM2.5-1.2B-Instruct BF16 on ROCm 10.0 decodes at **184.234** at depth 0 and **155.8044** at depth 65536 ([[benchmarks/llmb-r9700-rocm10-lfm25-1-2b-bf16-d0-decode]], [[benchmarks/llmb-r9700-rocm10-lfm25-1-2b-bf16-d64k-decode]]); prefill **20,717.405** ([[benchmarks/llmb-r9700-rocm10-lfm25-1-2b-bf16-d0-prefill]]). 184.234/32.0 = **5.76×** and 155.8044/32.0 = **4.87×** the 35B floor *(derived)*. 155.8044/184.234 = **0.846×** *(derived)*. The same file on the Strix Halo Vulkan path already cited is **74.49** ([[benchmarks/llmb-8060s-vulkan-lfm25-1-2b-bf16-d0-decode]]); 184.234/74.49 = **2.47×** *(derived)* — different silicon, different backend, a 1.2B BF16 file, so the multiple is not a speedup and it does not enter §4b. A 1.2B is the wrong byte count for the 20 GB denominator, same caveat the prior note already gives for this model on the 8060S.

List prices did not move. A hosted tok/s is not `memory_bandwidth_gbps / 20`, so it does not re-rank §3. The stretch goal this doc prices is still Llama-3.3-70B Q4 resident on a single device at 48 GB.
