That does not re-rank: the section prices one card against a 20 GB Q4, and this is a 20B-class MoE at Q8_0. Prefill sibling **1,041.18** ([[benchmarks/phx-vk-b7083-b580-gptoss20b-pp1024]]).
>
> - **RX 9070, unpriced, and the new Q8 reading is well under the Q4_0 cell already on file.** [[accelerators/amd-radeon-rx-9070]] is 16 GB / 640 GB/s, `-/-/-` (§4's does-not-fit set). Ceiling 640/20 = **32.0** tok/s *(derived)*. The dense tg128 cells are **69.23 / 72.72 / 67.8 / 69.23** ([[benchmarks/phx-vk-b7083-9070-dsr1-llama-8b-tg128]], [[benchmarks/phx-vk-b7083-9070-mistral-7b-tg128]], [[benchmarks/phx-vk-b7083-9070-qwen3-8b-tg128]], [[benchmarks/phx-vk-b7083-9070-tulu3-8b-tg128]]) → **2.16× / 2.27× / 2.12× / 2.16×** the 32.0 floor *(derived)* (72.72/32.0 = 2.273). The Vulkan #10879 Llama-2-7B Q4_0 cell already cited for this card is **119.51** ([[benchmarks/llamacpp-vulkan-rx-9070-llama27b-q40-fa-tg128]]), 119.51/32.0 = **3.73×** *(derived)*, and 119.51/72.72 = **1.64×** *(derived)*. Q4_0 versus Q8_0 on a 7–8B file, different build, so the gap is the quant and not a second protocol to average. gpt-oss-20b Q8_0 is **170.89** ([[benchmarks/phx-vk-b7083-9070-gptoss20b-tg128]]), 170.89/32.0 = **5.34×** and 170.89/72.72 = **2.35×** the best dense cell *(derived)*. Prefill siblings **2,821.94 / 2,829.08 / 2,754.84 / 2,823.06** and **3,531.8** for the MoE ([[benchmarks/phx-vk-b7083-9070-dsr1-llama-8b-pp1024]], [[benchmarks/phx-vk-b7083-9070-mistral-7b-pp1024]], [[benchmarks/phx-vk-b7083-9070-qwen3-8b-pp1024]], [[benchmarks/phx-vk-b7083-9070-tulu3-8b-pp1024]], [[benchmarks/phx-vk-b7083-9070-gptoss20b-pp1024]]). No purchase price, so §3 does not move.
>
> - **RX 9060 XT tracks the 9070 at half the bandwidth, and the new Q8 cell is half the Q4_0 one.** [[accelerators/amd-radeon-rx-9060-xt]] is 16 GB / 320 GB/s, `-/-/-`. Ceiling 320/20 = **16.0** tok/s *(derived)*. Dense tg128 is **37.04 / 38.95 / 36.53 / 37.04** ([[benchmarks/phx-vk-b7083-9060xt-dsr1-llama-8b-tg128]], [[benchmarks/phx-vk-b7083-9060xt-mistral-7b-tg128]], [[benchmarks/phx-vk-b7083-9060xt-qwen3-8b-tg128]], [[benchmarks/phx-vk-b7083-9060xt-tulu3-8b-tg128]]) → **2.32× / 2.43× / 2.28× / 2.32×** the 16.0 floor *(derived)* (38.95/16.0 = 2.434). The #10879 Q4_0 cell already on file is **74.01** ([[benchmarks/llamacpp-vulkan-rx-9060-xt-llama27b-q40-fa-tg128]]), 74.01/16.0 = **4.63×** and 74.01/38.95 = **1.90×** *(derived)* — the same Q4-versus-Q8 shape as the 9070, a little wider. gpt-oss-20b Q8_0 is **102.7** ([[benchmarks/phx-vk-b7083-9060xt-gptoss20b-tg128]]), 102.7/16.0 = **6.42×** and 102.7/38.95 = **2.64×** *(derived)*. The 9070 has exactly twice the bandwidth (640/320 = 2), and the dense decode ratio is 72.72/38.95 = **1.87×** *(derived)* — close to the bandwidth ratio, not equal to it. Prefill is further off: 2829.08/1792.13 = **1.58×** *(derived)* ([[benchmarks/phx-vk-b7083-9060xt-mistral-7b-pp1024]] and the other three dense pp1024 siblings at **1,787.9 / 1,745.08 / 1,788.06**, plus the MoE pp1024 at **2,319.17**, [[benchmarks/phx-vk-b7083-9060xt-gptoss20b-pp1024]]). The B580 has more bandwidth than the 9060 XT (456/320 = **1.43×** *(derived)*) and decodes the same dense Q8 file at 14.37/38.95 = **0.37×** *(derived)* — the bandwidth roofline does not predict a Vulkan-on-ANV result, which is why the rank prices the ceiling and not this cell.

> **Refresh note (docs/fit-matrix-oct14-p225, Oct-17, this pass).** Re-ran `python tools/fit_matrix_35b.py` on local `main` `77f4f6b` (226 accelerator records, 218 supply records, 4,112 benchmarks — #225 on top of `19d2ccc`). The selection rule still yields **159 qualifying / 43 priced / 116 unpriced** (69 of the unpriced fit 35B-A3B, 47 do not). Every priced cell was re-diffed against the generator: id, VRAM, bandwidth, the three fit flags, the 35B ceiling, the supply id, the price, the derived $/tok-s, and the order all match, so **the ranked table does not move** and no rank below is renumbered. #225 adds no accelerator and no purchase price. What it adds is a second box on a part §4 already lists, and a second reading of the rank-9 card plus the first time that card's stretch model is actually measured:
>
> - **L40S, still unpriced, and the new number is an 8-card aggregate, not a decode.** [[accelerators/nvidia-l40s]] stays 48 GB / 864 GB/s, **Y/Y/Y** (§4). The ceilings are 864/20 = **43.2**, 864/16 = **54.0**, 864/40 = **21.6** tok/s *(derived)*. #225 is HPE's MLPerf v5.0 closed submission, a DL380a Gen12 with 8× L40S and TensorRT-LLM, on llama2-70b-99.9 ([[sources/bench-mlperf-v5-0-hpe-dl380a-8xl40s-llama2-70b]]). Offline is **3,655.89** tok/s aggregate ([[benchmarks/mlperf-v5-0-hpe-dl380a-8xl40s-llama2-70b-offline-tokens]]) and Server is **3,181.65** ([[benchmarks/mlperf-v5-0-hpe-dl380a-8xl40s-llama2-70b-server-tokens]]), with p99 first-token latency **525.5** ms at the 11 qps target ([[benchmarks/mlperf-v5-0-hpe-dl380a-8xl40s-llama2-70b-server-ttft-p99]]). Dell's XE7745 in the same round, already on file, is **3,481.53** Offline and **3,201.13** Server ([[benchmarks/mlperf-v5-0-dell-xe7745-8xl40s-llama2-70b-offline-tokens]], [[benchmarks/mlperf-v5-0-dell-xe7745-8xl40s-llama2-70b-server-tokens]]). 3655.89/3481.53 = **1.05×** *(derived)* on Offline and 3181.65/3201.13 = **0.994×** *(derived)* on Server — the two boxes agree on Server and differ by about 5% on Offline, which is the commit's own reading. 3655.89/8 = **457.0** tok/s per card *(derived)*, and the record says that division is not a per-request decode. Set against the single-card llama.cpp Q4 cell already stored, **15.31** tok/s ([[benchmarks/xd-nvidia-l40s-70b-q4-k-m-tg1024]]), 15.31/21.6 = **0.71×** the 70B floor *(derived)* (15.31/21.6 = 0.709). 457.0/15.31 = **29.8×** *(derived)* — eight cards, a concurrent closed-division run, and a different engine, so the multiple is not a speedup and it does not enter §4b. No street price, so §3 does not move.
>
> - **RX 7900 XTX stays rank 9, and the new 7B cell is the low reading.** [[accelerators/amd-radeon-rx-7900-xtx]] is 24 GB / 960 GB/s, **Y/Y/-**, $875 → $18.23/tok-s *(derived)* ([[supply/ebay-used-rx-7900-xtx-market-oct2026]]). Ceilings: 960/20 = **48.0**, 960/16 = **60.0**, 960/40 = **24.0** tok/s *(derived)*. The card does not fit 70B. #225 is llama-bench build 34ce48d97, ROCm 7.1.1, one card of a 2× box, Llama-2-7B Q4_0, FA off: tg128 **118.82** and pp512 **3,487.15** ([[benchmarks/rocm711-rx7900xtx-gpu0-llama27b-q40-nofa-tg128]], [[benchmarks/rocm711-rx7900xtx-gpu0-llama27b-q40-nofa-pp512]], [[sources/gh-1337hero-rx7900xtx-llama-bench-rocm]]). The maintainer scoreboard on the same model, quant, and tg128 test is **167.11** ([[benchmarks/llamacpp-rocm-7900xtx-llama27b-q40-nofa-tg128]]). 167.11/118.82 = **1.41×** *(derived)*. The record keeps both: different build, different ROCm, and the two cards in this box already disagree (device 1 scored 130.67). Against the 20 GB ceiling, 118.82/48.0 = **2.48×** and 167.11/48.0 = **3.48×** *(derived)*. A 3.56 GiB file is the wrong byte count for that denominator, same caveat as the other Llama-2-7B scoreboard rows, so neither enters §4b and neither re-ranks the $18.23 cell. pp512 against the scoreboard's **3,552.27** ([[benchmarks/llamacpp-rocm-7900xtx-llama27b-q40-nofa-pp512]]) is 3487.15/3552.27 = **0.98×** *(derived)* — the gap is in decode, not prefill.
>
> - **The stretch model, split across two of the same card, lands under the 70B floor.** Llama 3.1 70B Q4_K_M is a 39.59 GiB file, so it does not fit the 24 GB the `Y/Y/-` flag describes. Split over both cards, FA on, the same build decodes at **13.37** tok/s ([[benchmarks/rocm711-2x-rx7900xtx-llama31-70b-q4km-fa-tg128]]). One card's 70B ceiling is 24.0, and 13.37/24.0 = **0.56×** *(derived)*. Two cards would be 48.0 if bandwidth added, and 13.37/48.0 = **0.28×** *(derived)* — it does not add. The link is host PCIe Gen4, which is the same failure the 2× tensor-split of Qwen3.8-27B Q4_K_XL already records at **47.06** tok/s on this pair ([[benchmarks/llamacpp-rx7900xtx-gfx1100-qwen3-27b-q4-batch1-decode-tok-s]]), 47.06/60.0 = **0.78×** one card's 27B floor *(derived)*. Different model, different build, same shape: the second card buys the fit and not the bandwidth. $875×2 / 13.37 = **$130.89 per measured tok/s** *(derived)* for a model the single card cannot hold. That is not a §3 rank — the section prices one card against the 20 GB ceiling, and this is two cards against a 39.59 GiB file.

> **Refresh note (docs/fit-matrix-oct14-p221, Oct-17, this pass).** Re-ran `python tools/fit_matrix_35b.py` on local `main` `5ba1be0` (226 accelerator records, 218 supply records, 4,106 benchmarks — #221 and #222 on top of `58a57fa`). The selection rule still yields **159 qualifying / 43 priced / 116 unpriced** (69 of the unpriced fit 35B-A3B, 47 do not). Every priced cell was re-diffed against the generator: id, VRAM, bandwidth, the three fit flags, the 35B ceiling, the supply id, the price, the derived $/tok-s, and the order all match, so **the ranked table does not move** and no rank below is renumbered. #221 adds no accelerator and no purchase price. #222 adds a model record, not a card. What #221 adds is the long-context half of a sweep §4b already cites at default context, on the same part:
>
> - **Strix Halo 8060S at 32k and 64k, and the named 35B-A3B Q4 cell holds.** [[accelerators/amd-radeon-8060s-ryzen-ai-max]] stays 128 GB unified / 256 GB/s, **Y/Y/Y**, rank 37 at $1,900 → $148.44/tok-s *(derived)* (35B ceiling 256/20 = **12.8** tok/s *(derived)*, 27B ceiling 256/16 = **16.0** tok/s *(derived)*). #221 is kyuz0's llama-bench long-context rows (llama.cpp b9193 / b9187, `-fa 1 -ngl 99 --mmap 0`, one request, tg32) from the same results.json the default-context filing used ([[sources/kyuz0-strix-halo-toolboxes-llamabench-results-json]]). Qwen3.6-35B-A3B UD-Q4_K_XL is a 21.27 GiB file. Vulkan RADV decodes at **49.21** tok/s at depth 32768 and **43.23** at depth 65536 ([[benchmarks/kyuz0-8060s-qwen3-6-35b-a3b-ud-q4-k-xl-radv-32k-tg32]], [[benchmarks/kyuz0-8060s-qwen3-6-35b-a3b-ud-q4-k-xl-radv-64k-tg32]]). Against the 12.8 floor that is **3.84× / 3.38×** *(derived)* (49.21/12.8 = 3.845, 43.23/12.8 = 3.377). The same file on ROCm 7 nightlies is **44.45** and **39.30** ([[benchmarks/kyuz0-8060s-qwen3-6-35b-a3b-ud-q4-k-xl-rocm7n-32k-tg32]], [[benchmarks/kyuz0-8060s-qwen3-6-35b-a3b-ud-q4-k-xl-rocm7n-64k-tg32]]) → **3.47× / 3.07×** *(derived)*. AMDVLK is **45.30** and **36.74** ([[benchmarks/kyuz0-8060s-qwen3-6-35b-a3b-ud-q4-k-xl-amdvlk-32k-tg32]], [[benchmarks/kyuz0-8060s-qwen3-6-35b-a3b-ud-q4-k-xl-amdvlk-64k-tg32]]) → **3.54× / 2.87×** *(derived)*. 43.23/49.21 = **0.88×** *(derived)* from 32k to 64k on RADV; 36.74/45.30 = **0.81×** *(derived)* on AMDVLK. None of these is the §4b row. That row is the default-context tg128 cell at **60.43** ([[benchmarks/kyuz0-strix-8060s-qwen36-35b-a3b-q4kxl-vulkan-radv-tg128]], 4.7×), and 49.21/60.43 = **0.81×** *(derived)* of it. tg32 at a primed 32k context is a different protocol from tg128 at the default depth, so it does not replace 60.43 and it does not replace the best-of-20 cell at **72.35**. $1,900/49.21 = **$38.61 per measured tok/s** *(derived)* at 32k and $1,900/43.23 = **$43.95** *(derived)* at 64k — both well above the rank-11 3090's derived $20.30 on the 20 GB ceiling, and a different context depth, so they do not re-rank. Prefill siblings, not a decode ceiling: RADV pp2048 is **715.52** at 32k and **524.7** at 64k ([[benchmarks/kyuz0-8060s-qwen3-6-35b-a3b-ud-q4-k-xl-radv-32k-pp2048]], [[benchmarks/kyuz0-8060s-qwen3-6-35b-a3b-ud-q4-k-xl-radv-64k-pp2048]]).
>
> - **The same box, BF16, falls through the 20 GB floor.** Qwen3.6-35B-A3B BF16 is a 64.6 GiB file, so the 20 GB denominator does not describe it. ROCm 7 nightlies still decodes at **23.96** tok/s at 32k and **22.60** at 64k ([[benchmarks/kyuz0-8060s-qwen3-6-35b-a3b-bf16-rocm7n-32k-tg32]], [[benchmarks/kyuz0-8060s-qwen3-6-35b-a3b-bf16-rocm7n-64k-tg32]]) → **1.87× / 1.77×** the 12.8 floor *(derived)* (23.96/12.8 = 1.872, 22.60/12.8 = 1.766). Vulkan does not: AMDVLK is **10.93** and **10.46** ([[benchmarks/kyuz0-8060s-qwen3-6-35b-a3b-bf16-amdvlk-32k-tg32]], [[benchmarks/kyuz0-8060s-qwen3-6-35b-a3b-bf16-amdvlk-64k-tg32]]) → **0.85× / 0.82×** *(derived)* (10.93/12.8 = 0.854, 10.46/12.8 = 0.817). 23.96/10.93 = **2.19×** *(derived)*, the same weights on two backends. Q8_K_XL sits between them: RADV **40.08** at 32k and **35.80** at 64k ([[benchmarks/kyuz0-8060s-qwen3-6-35b-a3b-ud-q8-k-xl-radv-32k-tg32]], [[benchmarks/kyuz0-8060s-qwen3-6-35b-a3b-ud-q8-k-xl-radv-64k-tg32]]) → **3.13× / 2.80×** *(derived)*.
>
> - **The dense-27B cell on this box is Q8, not Q4, and it misses the 16 GB floor.** Qwen3.6-27B UD-Q8_K_XL is a 33.31 GiB file. The best long-context decode is ROCm 6.4.4 at **6.13** tok/s at 32k and **5.81** at 64k ([[benchmarks/kyuz0-8060s-qwen3-6-27b-ud-q8-k-xl-rocm644-32k-tg32]], [[benchmarks/kyuz0-8060s-qwen3-6-27b-ud-q8-k-xl-rocm644-64k-tg32]]). Against the 16.0 floor that is **0.38× / 0.36×** *(derived)* (6.13/16.0 = 0.383, 5.81/16.0 = 0.363). RADV is **5.92** and **5.60** ([[benchmarks/kyuz0-8060s-qwen3-6-27b-ud-q8-k-xl-radv-32k-tg32]], [[benchmarks/kyuz0-8060s-qwen3-6-27b-ud-q8-k-xl-radv-64k-tg32]]); AMDVLK is **5.42** and **5.03** ([[benchmarks/kyuz0-8060s-qwen3-6-27b-ud-q8-k-xl-amdvlk-32k-tg32]], [[benchmarks/kyuz0-8060s-qwen3-6-27b-ud-q8-k-xl-amdvlk-64k-tg32]]). Q8 moves roughly twice the bytes the 16 GB denominator assumes, and the row is tg32 at 32k rather than a short-context Q4, so this is a bound on the floor, not a replacement for it. It does not enter §4b.
>
> - **Neighbours on the same sweep, none of them the 20 GB Q4 job.** Gemma-4-26B-A4B UD-Q4_K_XL (15.9 GiB) on RADV is **42.40** at 32k and **37.46** at 64k ([[benchmarks/kyuz0-8060s-gemma-4-26b-a4b-it-ud-q4-k-xl-radv-32k-tg32]], [[benchmarks/kyuz0-8060s-gemma-4-26b-a4b-it-ud-q4-k-xl-radv-64k-tg32]]) → **3.31× / 2.93×** the 12.8 floor *(derived)*. The dense sibling, Gemma-4-31B UD-Q4_K_XL (17.46 GiB), is **9.09** and **7.88** ([[benchmarks/kyuz0-8060s-gemma-4-31b-it-ud-q4-k-xl-radv-32k-tg32]], [[benchmarks/kyuz0-8060s-gemma-4-31b-it-ud-q4-k-xl-radv-64k-tg32]]) → **0.57× / 0.49×** the 16.0 floor *(derived)* (9.09/16.0 = 0.568, 7.88/16.0 = 0.493). gpt-oss-20b MXFP4 (11.27 GiB) is **61.12** and **50.13** ([[benchmarks/kyuz0-8060s-gpt-oss-20b-mxfp4-radv-32k-tg32]], [[benchmarks/kyuz0-8060s-gpt-oss-20b-mxfp4-radv-64k-tg32]]) → **4.78× / 3.92×** *(derived)*. gpt-oss-120b MXFP4 (59.02 GiB) is **43.01** and **34.97** ([[benchmarks/kyuz0-8060s-gpt-oss-120b-mxfp4-radv-32k-tg32]], [[benchmarks/kyuz0-8060s-gpt-oss-120b-mxfp4-radv-64k-tg32]]) → **3.36× / 2.73×** *(derived)*, a 120B-class file clearing the 20 GB floor because the active set is small. Qwen3.5-122B-A10B UD-Q5_K_XL (85.6 GiB) is **20.38** and **18.99** ([[benchmarks/kyuz0-8060s-qwen3-5-122b-a10b-ud-q5-k-xl-radv-32k-tg32]], [[benchmarks/kyuz0-8060s-qwen3-5-122b-a10b-ud-q5-k-xl-radv-64k-tg32]]) → **1.59× / 1.48×** *(derived)*. GLM-4.7-Flash UD-Q8_K_XL (32.7 GiB) is **22.55** and **15.70** ([[benchmarks/kyuz0-8060s-glm-4-7-flash-ud-q8-k-xl-radv-32k-tg32]], [[benchmarks/kyuz0-8060s-glm-4-7-flash-ud-q8-k-xl-radv-64k-tg32]]) → **1.76× / 1.23×** *(derived)*. Nemotron-3-Super-120B-A12B UD-Q4_K_XL (78.02 GiB) is **14.30** and **13.69** ([[benchmarks/kyuz0-8060s-nvidia-nemotron-3-super-120b-a12b-ud-q4-k-xl-radv-32k-tg32]], [[benchmarks/kyuz0-8060s-nvidia-nemotron-3-super-120b-a12b-ud-q4-k-xl-radv-64k-tg32]]) → **1.12× / 1.07×** *(derived)*. MiniMax-M2.7 UD-Q3_K_S (87.2 GiB) is **14.65** and **8.84** ([[benchmarks/kyuz0-8060s-minimax-m2-7-ud-q3-k-s-radv-32k-tg32]], [[benchmarks/kyuz0-8060s-minimax-m2-7-ud-q3-k-s-radv-64k-tg32]]) → **1.14× / 0.69×** *(derived)* (14.65/12.8 = 1.145, 8.84/12.8 = 0.691) — the one large-MoE row in this sweep that drops under the floor between 32k and 64k. The dense control, Llama-2-7B Q4_0 (3.56 GiB), is **9.42** at 32k and **5.12** at 64k on AMDVLK ([[benchmarks/kyuz0-8060s-llama-2-7b-q4-0-amdvlk-32k-tg32]], [[benchmarks/kyuz0-8060s-llama-2-7b-q4-0-amdvlk-64k-tg32]]), 9.42/12.8 = **0.74×** *(derived)*: a 7B file under a 20 GB ceiling is the KV, not the weights. None of these re-ranks §3.
>
> - **#222 is a model, not a row.** [[models/minicpm-v-4-7-35b-a3b]] is a 35.213B-parameter multimodal MoE with a vendor 3B activated figure, so it sits in the same nameplate class as the 35B-A3B workload this matrix prices. No benchmark and no accelerator landed with it, so it changes no fit flag and no $/tok-s.

> #223 re-reads OpenRouter's Llama-3.3-70B provider table. It moves no purchase price and no accelerator, so it does not enter this matrix.

> **Refresh note (docs/fit-matrix-oct14-p219, Oct-17, this pass).** Re-ran `python tools/fit_matrix_35b.py` on local `main` `d5c152f` (226 accelerator records, 218 supply records, 3,765 benchmarks — #219 on top of `6eb566d`). The selection rule still yields **159 qualifying / 43 priced / 116 unpriced** (69 of the unpriced fit 35B-A3B, 47 do not). Every priced cell was re-diffed against the generator: id, VRAM, bandwidth, the three fit flags, the 35B ceiling, the supply id, the price, the derived $/tok-s, and the order all match, so **the ranked table does not move** and no rank below is renumbered. #219 adds no accelerator and no purchase price. What it adds is a second protocol on a part §4 already lists, plus a two-card vLLM episode the single-card fit flag does not cover:
>
> - **2× R9700, and the new dense-27B row is FP8 at concurrency 8, not the Q4 batch-1 cell already in §4.** [[accelerators/amd-radeon-ai-pro-r9700]] stays 32 GB / 640 GB/s, **Y/Y/-**, unpriced (35B ceiling 640/20 = **32.0** tok/s *(derived)*, 27B ceiling 640/16 = **40.0** tok/s *(derived)*). §4 already quotes the single-card llama.cpp Q4 of this model: ROCm **30.66** tok/s, 0.77× the 40.0 floor ([[benchmarks/llmb-r9700-1gpu-rocm-qwen38-27b-q4kxl-d0-decode]]). #219 is vLLM 0.27.1 on **two** of the same card, Qwen3.8-27B **native FP8**, concurrency 8, per-stream, TTFT excluded, a ~2048-token follow-up and 256 generated tokens ([[sources/local-llm-benchmarks-r9700-episode-csv]]). Depth 0 decodes at **135.853** tok/s and depth 8192 at **116.822** ([[benchmarks/llmb-r9700-2x-vllm0271-qwen38-27b-fp8-c8-d0-decode]], [[benchmarks/llmb-r9700-2x-vllm0271-qwen38-27b-fp8-c8-d8k-decode]]). Against one card's 40.0 floor that is **3.40× / 2.92×** *(derived)* (135.853/40.0 = 3.396, 116.822/40.0 = 2.921). Both ratios use the wrong machine: two cards and a concurrent stream are not the single-device batch-1 the ceiling describes, and FP8 is not the Q4 the 16 GB denominator assumes. 116.822/135.853 = **0.86×** *(derived)* from depth 0 to 8k. Prefill siblings, not a decode ceiling: **3,411.146** and **2,291.275** ([[benchmarks/llmb-r9700-2x-vllm0271-qwen38-27b-fp8-c8-d0-prefill]], [[benchmarks/llmb-r9700-2x-vllm0271-qwen38-27b-fp8-c8-d8k-prefill]]). The record's own notes drop the 24k-and-deeper rows because the prefix cache collapsed, so those are not a depth curve. It does not enter §4b and it does not re-rank §3: no street price, and a 2-GPU FP8 concurrency-8 cell is not the A3B the section ranks. Set next to the single-card Q4, 135.853/30.66 = **4.43×** *(derived)* — two cards, a denser quant, eight streams, and a different engine, so the multiple is not a speedup.
>
> - **The other file is the Next/Flash class, and it only fits because the working set spills to NVMe.** Same two cards, vLLM, Qwen3.8-Flash-Next MXFP4/FP8/GPTQ with MTP-3 ([[models/qwen3-8-flash-next]]: 180B total, 6B active — not the 35B-A3B and not the 27B). Concurrency 1, TTFT excluded. Depth 0 decodes at **100.304** tok/s and depth 65536 at **106.684** ([[benchmarks/llmb-r9700-2x-tcclaviger-qwen38-flash-next-mtp3-d0-decode]], [[benchmarks/llmb-r9700-2x-tcclaviger-qwen38-flash-next-mtp3-d64k-decode]]). Against one card's 32.0 floor that is **3.13× / 3.33×** *(derived)* (100.304/32.0 = 3.134, 106.684/32.0 = 3.334). 106.684/100.304 = **1.06×** *(derived)* — the long context does not fall off, which is the MoE shape, on the wrong byte count and with speculative decoding on. The notes say the PLE rows live on NVMe (derived PLE file **41.60 GB**), so the working set is larger than the 64 GB the two cards hold and the `Y` on one 32 GB card does not cover this file. Prefill siblings: **2,676.443** at depth 0 and **1,116.002** at 64k ([[benchmarks/llmb-r9700-2x-tcclaviger-qwen38-flash-next-mtp3-d0-prefill]], [[benchmarks/llmb-r9700-2x-tcclaviger-qwen38-flash-next-mtp3-d64k-prefill]]). The sustained batch on the same build is aggregate, not per-request: **100.75 / 81.057 / 99.263 / 94.933 / 121.014** tok/s at c1-distinct, c2-distinct, c2-shared, c4-distinct, c4-shared ([[benchmarks/llmb-r9700-2x-tcclaviger-qwen38-flash-next-sustained-c1-distinct-tg]], [[benchmarks/llmb-r9700-2x-tcclaviger-qwen38-flash-next-sustained-c2-distinct-tg]], [[benchmarks/llmb-r9700-2x-tcclaviger-qwen38-flash-next-sustained-c2-shared-tg]], [[benchmarks/llmb-r9700-2x-tcclaviger-qwen38-flash-next-sustained-c4-distinct-tg]], [[benchmarks/llmb-r9700-2x-tcclaviger-qwen38-flash-next-sustained-c4-shared-tg]]). 121.014/100.75 = **1.20×** and 81.057/100.75 = **0.80×** *(derived)* — sharing the prefix helps and a second distinct stream does not, and the page says C8/C16 were not tested. None of it enters §4b.
>

> **Refresh note (docs/fit-matrix-oct14-p217, Oct-17, this pass).** Re-ran `python tools/fit_matrix_35b.py` on local `main` `84863d2` (226 accelerator records, 218 supply records, 3,752 benchmarks — #217 on top of `111572f`). The selection rule still yields **159 qualifying / 43 priced / 116 unpriced** (69 of the unpriced fit 35B-A3B, 47 do not). Every priced cell was re-diffed against the generator: id, VRAM, bandwidth, the three fit flags, the 35B ceiling, the supply id, the price, the derived $/tok-s, and the order all match, so **the ranked table does not move** and no rank below is renumbered. #217 adds no accelerator and no purchase price. What it adds is a context sweep on a part §4 already lists and §4b already quotes at short context:
>
> - **M5 Max, unpriced, and the new dense-27B row lands under the 27B ceiling the way a dense read does.** [[accelerators/local-apple-m5-max]] is 128 GB unified at **614 GB/s** (`Y/Y/Y`, §4). The 35B ceiling is 614/20 = **30.7** tok/s *(derived)* and the 27B ceiling is 614/16 = **38.4** tok/s *(derived, 614/16 = 38.375, printed 38.4)*. §4b already quotes the short-context A3B on this chip: MLX 4-bit **128.0** tok/s → 4.2× ([[benchmarks/ss-m5max-48-qwen35-35b-mlx-4bit-tg]]) and llama.cpp Q4_K_M **89.4** → 2.9× ([[benchmarks/ss-m5max-48-qwen35-35b-llamacpp-q4km-tg]]). #217 is a different protocol on the same 128 GB / 40-core machine: `mlx_lm.generate`, batch 1, one request, 128 generated tokens, version unprinted ([[sources/reddit-localllama-1rqnpvj-m5-max-128gb-mlx-lm-sweep]]). The file is Qwen3.5-27B-Claude-4.6-Opus-Distilled **MLX 6-bit**, not Q4, and it decodes at **23.648 / 20.311 / 14.908 / 14.225** tok/s at ~4k / 16k / 32k / 64k ([[benchmarks/m5max128-mlx-qwen35-27b-6bit-4k-tg]], [[benchmarks/m5max128-mlx-qwen35-27b-6bit-16k-tg]], [[benchmarks/m5max128-mlx-qwen35-27b-6bit-32k-tg]], [[benchmarks/m5max128-mlx-qwen35-27b-6bit-64k-tg]]). Against the 38.4 floor that is **0.62× / 0.53× / 0.39× / 0.37×** *(derived)* (23.648/38.375 = 0.616, 20.311/38.375 = 0.529, 14.908/38.375 = 0.388, 14.225/38.375 = 0.371). Under the floor at every depth, which is the same shape the 3090's Qwen3.5-27B Q4_K cell already shows at 0.57× (33.5/58.5) — different silicon, different quant, different engine, so the two ratios are not a ranking. 6-bit reads more bytes than the Q4 the floor assumes, so 0.62× is a lower bound on a Q4 reading of this file, not a Q4 measurement. Peak memory on the 4k row is **25.319 GB**, above the 16 GB weight the ceiling divides by, which is why the file still clears the 20 GB fit bar and why the ratio sits where a dense read sits. 23.648/14.225 = **1.66×** *(derived)* from 4k to 64k; the prefill siblings fall the same way, **811.134 → 475.828** ([[benchmarks/m5max128-mlx-qwen35-27b-6bit-4k-pp]], [[benchmarks/m5max128-mlx-qwen35-27b-6bit-64k-pp]], 811.134/475.828 = **1.70×** *(derived)*). It does not enter §4b: a 6-bit dense 27B is not the A3B the section ranks, and there is still no purchase price, so §3 does not move. Set next to the MLX A3B cell already on this chip, 128.0/23.648 = **5.41×** *(derived)* — the MoE-vs-dense gap, on one machine, at two different context depths.
>
> - **The other three files are larger than the 20 GB denominator, and two of them are the MoE class this doc is about.** Same machine, same `mlx_lm.generate` protocol. Qwen3.5-122B-A10B MLX 4-bit decodes at **60.639** at ~16k and **54.923** at ~32k ([[benchmarks/m5max128-mlx-qwen35-122b-4bit-16k-tg]], [[benchmarks/m5max128-mlx-qwen35-122b-4bit-32k-tg]]; peak **73.803 / 76.397 GB**). Against the 30.7 floor that is **1.98× / 1.79×** *(derived)* (60.639/30.7 = 1.975, 54.923/30.7 = 1.789). There is no 4k cell for this file. Qwen3-Coder-Next MLX 8-bit — the Next/Flash class, not a 35B-A3B — decodes at **74.293 / 68.624 / 48.212** at ~16k / 32k / 64k ([[benchmarks/m5max128-mlx-qwen3-coder-next-8bit-16k-tg]], [[benchmarks/m5max128-mlx-qwen3-coder-next-8bit-32k-tg]], [[benchmarks/m5max128-mlx-qwen3-coder-next-8bit-64k-tg]]; peak **88.176 / 89.652 / 92.605 GB**) → **2.42× / 2.24× / 1.57×** *(derived)* (74.293/30.7 = 2.420, 68.624/30.7 = 2.235, 48.212/30.7 = 1.570). It stays above the dense-equivalent floor out to 64k, which is the MoE shape §4b already records, on a larger active model and an 8-bit file. gpt-oss-120b MXFP4-Q8 decodes at **87.873 / 75.963 / 64.469** at ~4k / 16k / 32k ([[benchmarks/m5max128-mlx-gptoss-120b-mxfp4-q8-4k-tg]], [[benchmarks/m5max128-mlx-gptoss-120b-mxfp4-q8-16k-tg]], [[benchmarks/m5max128-mlx-gptoss-120b-mxfp4-q8-32k-tg]]; peak **64.408 / 64.857 / 65.461 GB**) → **2.86× / 2.47× / 2.10×** *(derived)* (87.873/30.7 = 2.862, 75.963/30.7 = 2.474, 64.469/30.7 = 2.100). 120B is not the 27B class and MXFP4-Q8 is not the 20 GB Q4, so the multiple is the wrong byte count; it is the file that uses half the 128 GB pool and it does not enter §4b either. Prefill siblings, not a decode ceiling: 122B **1,239.734 / 1,067.824** ([[benchmarks/m5max128-mlx-qwen35-122b-4bit-16k-pp]], [[benchmarks/m5max128-mlx-qwen35-122b-4bit-32k-pp]]), Coder-Next **1,802.144 / 1,887.158 / 1,432.73** ([[benchmarks/m5max128-mlx-qwen3-coder-next-8bit-16k-pp]], [[benchmarks/m5max128-mlx-qwen3-coder-next-8bit-32k-pp]], [[benchmarks/m5max128-mlx-qwen3-coder-next-8bit-64k-pp]]), gpt-oss-120b **1,325.062 / 2,710.46 / 2,537.42** ([[benchmarks/m5max128-mlx-gptoss-120b-mxfp4-q8-4k-pp]], [[benchmarks/m5max128-mlx-gptoss-120b-mxfp4-q8-16k-pp]], [[benchmarks/m5max128-mlx-gptoss-120b-mxfp4-q8-32k-pp]]). No street price on the M5 Max, so none of the four files produces a $/tok-s cell.

> **Refresh note (docs/fit-matrix-oct14-p215, Oct-17, this pass).** Re-ran `python tools/fit_matrix_35b.py` on local `main` `c45efa7` (226 accelerator records, 218 supply records, 3,728 benchmarks — #214 and #215 on top of `2ae5d01`). The selection rule now yields **159 qualifying / 43 priced / 116 unpriced** (69 of the unpriced fit 35B-A3B, 47 do not). Every priced cell was re-diffed against the generator: id, VRAM, bandwidth, the three fit flags, the 35B ceiling, the supply id, the price, the derived $/tok-s, and the order all match, so **the ranked table does not move** and no rank below is renumbered. #214 adds sixteen accelerators and 123 llama.cpp scoreboard rows; #215 re-reads six service rows. None of the new accelerators has a purchase-price supply record, so the priced set stays 43.
>
> - **Five of the sixteen clear the gate. One fits 35B-A3B, and the fit is the board, not the GPU the benchmark measured.** [[accelerators/nvidia-tesla-k80]] is a dual-GK210 board, `vram_gb` **24** and `memory_bandwidth_gbps` **480** (the record's own basis: 240 GB/s and 12 GB per GK210, summed). 24 ≥ 20+4 and 24 ≥ 16+4, so the flags are **Y/Y/-** and the ceilings are 480/20 = **24.0**, 480/16 = **30.0**, 480/40 = **12.0** tok/s *(derived)*. It enters §4 between [[accelerators/huawei-ascend-950dt]] (25.0) and [[accelerators/huawei-atlas-300i-duo]] (20.4). The record's notes say the scoreboard row is one GPU of the pair, and the benchmark notes print **12 GB / 384-bit**. One GK210 is 12 GB, which misses the 24 GB bar, so the `Y` is a board total a single context cannot see. No purchase price.
> - **The other four clear the bandwidth floor and fit nothing.** All `-/-/-`, all unpriced, all in the does-not-fit list: [[accelerators/nvidia-titan-xp]] (12 GB, 547.7 GB/s, 35B ceiling 547.7/20 = **27.4** tok/s *(derived)*, between the Arc A770 at 28.0 and the RX 6800 XT at 25.6), [[accelerators/amd-instinct-mi25]] (16 GB HBM2, 484 GB/s, ceiling **24.2** *(derived)* — tied with [[accelerators/amd-radeon-rx-vega-64]], and the record notes that ROCm support for gfx900 ended years ago), [[accelerators/nvidia-rtx-2060-super]] (8 GB, 448 GB/s, ceiling **22.4** *(derived)* — tied with the RX 5700 XT, the RTX 2070 SUPER / 3060 Ti / 3070, the RTX 5060 Ti and the RTX A4000) and [[accelerators/nvidia-quadro-rtx-4000]] (8 GB, 416 GB/s, ceiling **20.8** *(derived)*, between the RX 6750 XT at 21.6 and the RX Vega 56 at 20.5). 27B ceilings are 547.7/16 = **34.2**, 484/16 = **30.3**, 448/16 = **28.0**, 416/16 = **26.0** tok/s *(derived)*; none of the four reaches the 20 GB the 27B column needs.
> - **The other eleven never reach the gate.** Below 8 GB: [[accelerators/nvidia-gtx-1060-6gb]], [[accelerators/nvidia-gtx-1660]], [[accelerators/nvidia-p106-100]] and [[accelerators/nvidia-rtx-4050-laptop]] (all 6 GB), [[accelerators/nvidia-quadro-p2000]] (5 GB), [[accelerators/nvidia-quadro-p1000]] and [[accelerators/nvidia-quadro-t1000]] (both 4 GB). At or above 8 GB but under 300 GB/s and with no `<= $3,000` purchase price: [[accelerators/nvidia-gtx-1070-ti]] (8 GB, 256 GB/s), [[accelerators/nvidia-jetson-agx-orin-64gb]] (64 GB unified LPDDR5, 204.8 GB/s) and [[accelerators/nvidia-tesla-p4]] (8 GB, 192 GB/s). [[accelerators/amd-radeon-880m]] is `unified_memory` with both `vram_gb` and `memory_bandwidth_gbps` null, and it is not in the generator's configured-pool override, so the gate drops it. None enters §3 or §4.
> - **Every new scoreboard cell is Llama-2-7B Q4_0, 3.56 GiB, not the 35B-A3B.** llama-bench, pp512/tg128, batch 1, full offload, per-request ([[sources/llama-cpp-discussion-15013-nvidia-cuda-scoreboard]], and the ROCm half [[sources/llama-cpp-discussion-15021-amd-rocm-scoreboard]]). 3.56/20 = **0.18** of the weight the ceiling divides by *(derived)*, so a ratio above 1× is the small-file case, not evidence the 20 GB model runs. The five that cleared the gate, FA where both exist: K80 **14.27** tok/s ([[benchmarks/llamacpp-scoreboard-tesla-k80-fa-tg128]], no-FA **13.80** [[benchmarks/llamacpp-scoreboard-tesla-k80-nofa-tg128]]). 14.27/24.0 = **0.59×** the board ceiling *(derived)* and 13.80/24.0 = **0.58×**; 14.27/13.80 = **1.03×** *(derived)*. Read against one GK210 the same cells are 14.27/12.0 = **1.19×** and 13.80/12.0 = **1.15×** *(derived, 240/20 = 12.0)* — still a 7B file, and the prefill pair is 133.36 vs 133.14, **1.00×** *(derived)*. TITAN Xp no-FA **76.08** ([[benchmarks/llamacpp-scoreboard-titan-xp-nofa-tg128]], FA **73.84** [[benchmarks/llamacpp-scoreboard-titan-xp-fa-tg128]]): 76.08/27.4 = **2.78×** *(derived)*, 73.84/27.4 = **2.70×**, 76.08/73.84 = **1.03×**. MI25 has no FA row: **63.94** ([[benchmarks/llamacpp-scoreboard-instinct-mi25-nofa-tg128]]), 63.94/24.2 = **2.64×** *(derived)*. RTX 2060 SUPER FA **61.13** ([[benchmarks/llamacpp-scoreboard-rtx-2060-super-fa-tg128]], no-FA **60.04** [[benchmarks/llamacpp-scoreboard-rtx-2060-super-nofa-tg128]]): 61.13/22.4 = **2.73×**, 60.04/22.4 = **2.68×**, 61.13/60.04 = **1.02×** *(derived)*. Quadro RTX 4000 FA **67.62** ([[benchmarks/llamacpp-scoreboard-quadro-rtx-4000-fa-tg128]], no-FA **65.62** [[benchmarks/llamacpp-scoreboard-quadro-rtx-4000-nofa-tg128]]): 67.62/20.8 = **3.25×**, 65.62/20.8 = **3.15×**, 67.62/65.62 = **1.03×** *(derived)*. None of these is an A3B measurement, so none enters §4b, and none has a price, so none re-ranks §3.

> **Refresh note (docs/fit-matrix-oct14, Oct-17, this pass).** Re-ran `python tools/fit_matrix_35b.py` on local `main` `2e3d05f` (210 accelerator records, 218 supply records, 3,605 benchmarks — #211 and #212 on top of `add09bf`). The selection rule still yields **154 qualifying / 43 priced / 111 unpriced** (68 of the unpriced fit 35B-A3B, 43 do not). Every priced cell was re-diffed against the generator: id, VRAM, bandwidth, the three fit flags, the 35B ceiling, the supply id, the price, the derived $/tok-s, and the order all match, so **the ranked table does not move** and no rank below is renumbered. #211 adds a model record and fills one active-param field; #212 re-reads six service rows. Neither adds an accelerator, a supply row, or a benchmark, so the gate is the same 154 the previous note counted.
>
> - **The new model is the dense 27B this table already uses 16 GB for, and it does not change the byte count.** [[models/qwen3-8-27b]] is Qwen3.8-27B, dense hybrid, `params_b` **27.781** and `active_params_b` **27.781** (safetensors total 27,781,427,952 BF16; the card rounds it to 27B). The 27B column stays `weights = 16 GB` + 4 GB KV, needing `vram_gb >= 20`. Nothing in the new record is a measured Q4 file size, so the 16 GB assumption is not revised here. The card's own comparison (Terminal Bench 2.1 73.0 vs 63.4, SWE-bench Pro 61.7 vs 53.5, GPQA Diamond 89.2 vs 87.8 against Qwen3.6-27B) is a quality claim, not a bandwidth one, and it does not enter §3.
> - **The Q4 measurement of that model was already on file, on a part §3 already prices.** [[accelerators/apple-m3-max]] is rank 39 (128 GB, 400 GB/s, 27B ceiling 400/16 = **25.0** tok/s *(derived)*, $3,400 → $170.00/tok-s *(derived)* from [[supply/used-macbook-pro-m3-max-128gb]]). Ollama's llama.cpp backend runs Qwen3.8-27B Q4_K_M at **12.0** tok/s ([[benchmarks/ollama-m3-max-qwen3-8-27b-llamacpp-decode-tok-s]]). 12.0/25.0 = **0.48×** that ceiling *(derived)* — under the floor, the same shape the M2 Max IQ3_S cell already shows at 0.43× and the R9700 UD-Q4_K_XL cell at 0.77×, on different silicon and different protocols, so the three ratios are not a ranking. The MLX sibling on the same machine is **42.5** tok/s ([[benchmarks/ollama-m3-max-qwen3-8-27b-mlx-decode-tok-s]]), `format_id` null, methodology says NVFP4. 42.5/25.0 = **1.70×** *(derived)*, and 42.5/12.0 = **3.54×** *(derived)* between the two backends. NVFP4 is not Q4_K_M, so the 1.70× is not a Q4 reading and it does not enter §4b. $3,400/12.0 = **$283.33 per measured Q4 tok/s** *(derived)* — not a re-rank; the ranking stays on the 20 GB ceiling.
> - **GLM-5.3's filled active-param field is the wrong model class.** [[models/glm-5-3]] is 753.3B total with `active_params_b` now **40** (the card's figure; the record's notes keep the 40.93B config arithmetic beside it). A 40B-active MoE is not the 35B-A3B, the 27B dense, or the 70B dense this table fits, and the fill adds no accelerator and no price.
> - **#212 moves hosted tok/s, which this table does not rank.** The OpenRouter re-read moves the Groq row on Llama 3.3 70B from 0.50 s / 198 tok/s to 0.49 s / 209 tok/s, and the DeepInfra, Novita, SambaNova, Vertex and Together P50s move in notes. Those are service records ([[services/svc-openrouter]], [[services/svc-deepinfra]], [[services/svc-novita]], [[services/svc-sambanova-cloud]], [[services/svc-gcp-vertex]], [[services/svc-together-serverless]]), not purchase prices, so no $/tok-s cell changes.

> **Refresh note (docs/fit-matrix-oct17-p11, Oct-17, this pass).** Re-ran `python tools/fit_matrix_35b.py` on local `main` `42edb33` (210 accelerator records, 218 supply records, 3,605 benchmarks — #209 on top of `c9ca28e`). The selection rule still yields **154 qualifying / 43 priced / 111 unpriced** (68 of the unpriced fit 35B-A3B, 43 do not). Every priced cell was re-diffed against the generator: id, VRAM, bandwidth, the three fit flags, the 35B ceiling, the supply id, the price, the derived $/tok-s, and the order all match, so **the ranked table does not move** and no rank below is renumbered. #209 adds one accelerator and no purchase price. The new chip does not clear the gate, and the one new row that is the named 35B-A3B target sits on a part §4 already lists:
>
> - **Base M4 does not qualify.** [[accelerators/local-apple-m4]] is a Mac mini base M4, 24 GB unified LPDDR5X at **120 GB/s**. 24 GB meets the 35B-A3B bar and the 27B bar and misses the 48 GB 70B bar (`Y/Y/-`), but 120 GB/s misses the 300 GB/s bandwidth floor, and there is no purchase-price supply record at `<= $3,000` (no `data/supply/` row names this id), so the selection rule keeps it out of the 154 — the same shape as GB10 and Jetson AGX Thor, at less than half their bandwidth. The arithmetic the ceiling column uses gives 120/20 = **6.0** tok/s *(derived)*, 120/16 = **7.5** tok/s *(derived)* on the 27B column, and 120/40 = **3.0** tok/s *(derived)* on the 70B column. It does not enter §3 or §4. The record's own note says the comment does not print a GPU-core count, so this is the base M4 and not the M4 Pro or the M4 Max already ranked at 40.
> - **The two rows on that chip are the wrong byte count.** llama.cpp discussion #4167, Metal, build 2e97c5f, batch 1: llama 3.2 3B Q4_K_M (1.87 GiB) tg128 **45.95** ([[benchmarks/llamacpp-metal-m4-base-llama32-3b-q4km-tg128-2e97c5f]]) and Qwen2.5-Coder 7B Q4_K_M (4.36 GiB) tg128 **22.60** ([[benchmarks/llamacpp-metal-m4-base-qwen25-coder-7b-q4km-tg128-2e97c5f]]). Against the 6.0 floor those are 45.95/6.0 = **7.66×** and 22.60/6.0 = **3.77×** *(derived)* — a 1.87 GiB file and a 4.36 GiB file, not a 20 GB one, so the multiples are small-model calibrations and not evidence the 120 GB/s pool decodes the 35B-A3B at those rates. Neither enters §4b. No purchase price, so no $/tok-s cell.
> - **M2 Max, already in §4, now has the named 35B-A3B at Q4, and the chip in the comment is the slower SKU.** [[accelerators/apple-m2-max]] (96 GB, record bandwidth **400 GB/s**, 35B ceiling 400/20 = **20.0** tok/s *(derived)*, **Y/Y/Y**, unpriced). The same discussion, comment 18555918, is an M2 Max **30-core GPU, 32 GB**, not the 38-core / 96 GB the record's bandwidth is published for — the record's own basis says the 30-core configuration is listed at **300 GB/s**. llama-bench, batch 1, build unprinted: Qwen3.6-35B-A3B Q4_K_M (20.22 GiB) decodes at **58.56** tok/s tg128 ([[benchmarks/llamacpp-metal-m2-max-30core-qwen36-35b-a3b-q4km-tg128]]) and prefills at **671.4** tok/s pp4096 ([[benchmarks/llamacpp-metal-m2-max-30core-qwen36-35b-a3b-q4km-pp4096]]). Against the record's 20.0 floor that is 58.56/20.0 = **2.93×** *(derived)*. Against the 300 GB/s the comment's own SKU is listed at, 300/20 = **15.0** tok/s *(derived)* and 58.56/15.0 = **3.90×** *(derived)*. The 20.22 GiB file is the byte count the floor assumes, which is why this row enters §4b and the 7B rows above do not. It does not enter §3: still no purchase price, and the 32 GB machine is not the 96 GB the fit flag is computed against — 20.22 GiB of weights plus the OS on 32 GB is the record's own "context headroom is small" caveat, not a 70B fit.
> - **The dense sibling on the same comment lands under the floor, and it is the wrong quant.** Qwen3.8-27B IQ3_S (11.20 GiB) decodes at **10.82** tok/s ([[benchmarks/llamacpp-metal-m2-max-30core-qwen38-27b-iq3s-tg128]]) and prefills at **106.78** ([[benchmarks/llamacpp-metal-m2-max-30core-qwen38-27b-iq3s-pp4096]]). The 27B ceiling on the record is 400/16 = **25.0** tok/s *(derived)*; 10.82/25.0 = **0.43×** *(derived)*. IQ3_S is not Q4 and 11.20 GiB is not 16 GB, so the ratio is a smaller file than the denominator, which makes 0.43× a lower bound on how far a Q4 dense 27B would sit under the floor, not a Q4 measurement. The MoE-vs-dense gap on one comment is 58.56/10.82 = **5.41×** *(derived)* at decode and 671.4/106.78 = **6.29×** *(derived)* at prefill — the same shape the 3090 note already records (179.0 vs 33.5 at 4k), now on a 400 GB/s unified pool. The comment's MTP sweep is an ollama draft-head run and is not these rows.
> - **The rest of #209 is Llama-2-7B, so it calibrates and does not re-rank.** On the priced M1 Ultra (rank 28, 800 GB/s, ceiling **40.0** tok/s *(derived)*): Q4_0 tg128 **113.79** ([[benchmarks/llamacpp-metal-m1-ultra-64core-q4-0-tg128-c1d0e7a]]), 113.79/40.0 = **2.84×** *(derived)* — a 7B file, not a 20 GB one. On the priced M3 Max (rank 39, 400 GB/s, ceiling **20.0** *(derived)*, tied with the M2 Max): Q4_0 tg128 **81.57** ([[benchmarks/llamacpp-metal-m3-max-40core-q4-0-tg128-c1d0e7a]]), 81.57/20.0 = **4.08×** *(derived)*, same caveat. The M5 / M5 Pro / M5 Max / M5 Ultra cells in the same ingest are the same 7B protocol on parts §4 already lists. None of them is an A3B, so none enters §4b, and none carries a purchase price, so §3 does not move.

> **Refresh note (docs/fit-matrix-oct17-p10, Oct-17, this pass).** Re-ran `python tools/fit_matrix_35b.py` on local `main` `c9ca28e` (209 accelerator records, 218 supply records, 3,549 benchmarks — #206 on top of `5bebcc4`). The selection rule now yields **154 qualifying / 43 priced / 111 unpriced** (68 of the unpriced fit 35B-A3B, 43 do not). Every priced cell was re-diffed against the generator: id, VRAM, bandwidth, the three fit flags, the 35B ceiling, the supply id, the price, the derived $/tok-s, and the order all match, so **the ranked table does not move** and no rank below is renumbered. #206 adds two accelerators and no purchase price. Both clear the bandwidth floor and fit none of the three workloads, so both land in the does-not-fit list and neither enters §3 or §4:
>
> - **Arc A750 qualifies and does not fit.** [[accelerators/intel-arc-a750]] is 8 GB GDDR6 at **512 GB/s**, 225 W. 8 GB misses the 24 GB 35B-A3B bar, the 20 GB 27B bar and the 48 GB 70B bar (`-/-/-`); 512 GB/s clears the 300 GB/s floor, which is why it qualifies with no purchase price. Ceiling 512/20 = **25.6** tok/s *(derived)* — tied with [[accelerators/amd-radeon-rx-6800]] / [[accelerators/amd-radeon-rx-6800-xt]] / [[accelerators/amd-radeon-rx-6900-xt]], which are 16 GB at the same bus. The 27B column is 512/16 = **32.0** tok/s *(derived)* and the 70B column is 512/40 = **12.8** tok/s *(derived)*. The only measured rows are llama-2-7b Q4_0 (3.56 GiB), batch-1, Vulkan, build c1b1876: noFA pp512 **1,075.94** / tg128 **42.66** ([[benchmarks/llamacpp-vulkan-arc-a750-llama27b-q40-nofa-pp512]], [[benchmarks/llamacpp-vulkan-arc-a750-llama27b-q40-nofa-tg128]]) and FA pp512 **303.37** / tg128 **43.96** ([[benchmarks/llamacpp-vulkan-arc-a750-llama27b-q40-fa-pp512]], [[benchmarks/llamacpp-vulkan-arc-a750-llama27b-q40-fa-tg128]]). Against the 25.6 floor the faster decode is 43.96/25.6 = **1.72×** *(derived)* — a 3.56 GiB file, not a 20 GB one, so the multiple is a small-model calibration and not evidence the 8 GB card holds the 24 GB model. FA regresses prefill hard on this chip (1,075.94/303.37 = **3.55×** *(derived)* slower); decode barely moves (43.96/42.66 = **1.03×** *(derived)*). Neither row enters §4b.
> - **Radeon Pro W5700 qualifies and does not fit.** [[accelerators/amd-radeon-pro-w5700]] is 8 GB GDDR6 at **448 GB/s**, 205 W, RDNA1 Navi 10. Same `-/-/-` miss; 448 GB/s clears the floor. Ceiling 448/20 = **22.4** tok/s *(derived)* — tied with [[accelerators/amd-radeon-rx-5700-xt]], [[accelerators/nvidia-rtx-2070-super]], [[accelerators/nvidia-rtx-3060-ti]], [[accelerators/nvidia-rtx-3070]] and [[accelerators/nvidia-rtx-5060-ti]]. The 27B column is 448/16 = **28.0** tok/s *(derived)* and the 70B column is 448/40 = **11.2** tok/s *(derived)*. llama-2-7b Q4_0, batch-1, Vulkan, build 23bc779: noFA pp512 **449.85** / tg128 **68.55** ([[benchmarks/llamacpp-vulkan-radeon-pro-w5700-llama27b-q40-nofa-pp512]], [[benchmarks/llamacpp-vulkan-radeon-pro-w5700-llama27b-q40-nofa-tg128]]) and FA pp512 **446.98** / tg128 **71.30** ([[benchmarks/llamacpp-vulkan-radeon-pro-w5700-llama27b-q40-fa-pp512]], [[benchmarks/llamacpp-vulkan-radeon-pro-w5700-llama27b-q40-fa-tg128]]). Against the 22.4 floor the faster decode is 71.30/22.4 = **3.18×** *(derived)*, again a 3.56 GiB file. FA is a wash on prefill (449.85/446.98 = **1.01×** *(derived)*) and a small decode gain (71.30/68.55 = **1.04×** *(derived)*). The W5700X (40 CU, 16 GB) is a different board and is not this record. Neither row enters §4b. No purchase price on either card, so no $/tok-s cell.

> **Refresh note (docs/fit-matrix-oct17-p9, Oct-17, this pass).** Re-ran `python tools/fit_matrix_35b.py` on local `main` `6b863a4` (207 accelerator records, 218 supply records, 3,541 benchmarks — #203 on top of `e68a507`). The selection rule still yields **152 qualifying / 43 priced / 109 unpriced** (68 of the unpriced fit 35B-A3B, 41 do not). Every priced cell was re-diffed against the generator: id, VRAM, bandwidth, the three fit flags, the 35B ceiling, the supply id, the price, the derived $/tok-s, and the order all match, so **the ranked table does not move** and no rank below is renumbered. #203 adds one accelerator and no purchase price. The new card does not clear the gate:
>
> - **Arc Pro B50 does not qualify.** [[accelerators/intel-arc-pro-b50]] is 16 GB GDDR6 at **224 GB/s**, 70 W, half-height. 16 GB misses the 24 GB 35B-A3B bar and the 20 GB 27B bar (`-/-/-`), and 224 GB/s misses the 300 GB/s bandwidth floor. There is no purchase-price supply record at `<= $3,000` (no `data/supply/` row names this id), so the selection rule keeps it out of the 152 — the same shape as RTX PRO 2000 Blackwell, except that card clears the bandwidth floor and this one does not. The arithmetic the ceiling column uses gives 224/20 = **11.2** tok/s *(derived)*, 224/16 = **14.0** tok/s *(derived)* on the 27B column, and 224/40 = **5.6** tok/s *(derived)* on the 70B column. It does not enter §3 or §4. The record's own note says a 4-bit 14B fits and a 4-bit 32B dense does not, which is the same 16 GB miss the fit flags encode.
> - **The two new rows are a 4-card aggregate, and they are the wrong byte count.** MLPerf Inference v6.0 closed, PyTorch vLLM, Llama-3.1-8B INT8, vendor-measured on 4× MAXSUN Arc Pro B50: Offline **1,721.84** tok/s aggregate ([[benchmarks/b50-4x-mlperf-v6-0-llama31-8b-offline-tokens]]) and Server **1,187.13** tok/s aggregate ([[benchmarks/b50-4x-mlperf-v6-0-llama31-8b-server-tokens]]). The record says the submission does not publish a per-card split; dividing by 4 is an average only, 1,721.84/4 = **430.46** and 1,187.13/4 = **296.78** tok/s per card *(derived)*. Against the 11.2 floor those are **38.4×** and **26.5×** *(derived)* (430.46/11.2 = 38.43, 296.78/11.2 = 26.50) — an INT8 8B, not a Q4 20 GB file, so the multiple is a small-model calibration and not evidence the 16 GB card holds the 24 GB model. Offline is a throughput scenario and Server is latency-bounded; neither is a batch-1 decode, and neither enters §4b. The same submitter's 4× Arc Pro B60 Offline row on Llama-3.1-8B FP8 is **3,347.43** ([[benchmarks/b60-4x-mlperf-v6-0-llama31-8b-offline-tokens]]); 3,347.43/1,721.84 = **1.94×** *(derived)*, different silicon, different quant (FP8 vs INT8), same 4-card shape, not a re-rank. [[accelerators/intel-arc-pro-b60]] stays at rank 16 ($650, 24 GB, **Y/Y/-**). No purchase price on the B50, so no $/tok-s cell.

Against the 77.8 floor those are **0.87× / 0.54× / 0.57× / 0.11×** *(derived)*. An aggregate per-stream rate is not a batch-1 decode, and none of the four is the 35B-A3B the §4b row already quotes at **136** tok/s ([[benchmarks/llamabench-a100pcie40-qwen36-35b-a3b-q4kxl-tg128]], 1.7× that same floor). None of the four enters §4b.
> - **A40 stays unpriced, and the new rows are the same aggregate shape the previous note already recorded.** [[accelerators/nvidia-a40]] (48 GB, 696 GB/s, 35B ceiling 696/20 = **34.8** tok/s *(derived)*, **Y/Y/Y**, §4). Same vLLM page, six model rows the earlier filing did not have. The 4B row going from 15.13 per stream at 50 concurrent to 4.01 at 100 is a saturation drop, not a bandwidth reading. All eleven are the wrong byte count for a 20 GB Q4, and the 16B cell sitting at 1.02× the 20 GB floor is a coincidence of denominators, not evidence the 16 GB card holds the 24 GB model. gemma2:27b evals at **2.38** ([[benchmarks/dm-a4000-ollama-gemma-2-27b-eval]]). 2.38/28.0 = **0.09×** the 27B ceiling *(derived)*, and the record flags it: 1% GPU, 70–86% CPU, the weight does not fit in 16 GB. That is a spill, not a dense-27B measurement, and it does not enter §4b. The Vulkan #10879 Llama-2-7B Q4_0 cell already cited for this card is **76.07** ([[benchmarks/llamacpp-vulkan-rtx-a4000-llama27b-q40-fa-tg128]]); 65.06 against 76.07 is a free-length default pull versus a fixed tg128, not a second protocol to average. No purchase price.

Every priced cell was re-diffed against the generator: id, VRAM, bandwidth, the three fit flags, the 35B ceiling, the supply id, the price, the derived $/tok-s, and the order all match, so **the ranked table does not move** and no rank below is renumbered. #197 adds no accelerator and no purchase price. What it adds is the first MLPerf LLM rows and the first vendor vLLM table for a part this doc already keeps outside the gate:

> - **Jetson AGX Thor still does not qualify, and the new vendor row is a Qwen3-30B-A3B decode on the same 273 GB/s bus.** [[accelerators/nvidia-jetson-agx-thor]] is 128 GB unified LPDDR5X at **273 GB/s**. That clears the 24 GB fit bar and misses the 300 GB/s bandwidth floor, and there is still no purchase-price supply record at `<= $3,000` (the record's `accelerator_ids` are joined to nothing in `data/supply/`), so the selection rule keeps it out of the 152. The same arithmetic the ceiling column uses gives 273/20 = **13.7** tok/s *(derived)*, 273/16 = **17.1** tok/s *(derived)* on the 27B column, and 273/40 = **6.8** tok/s *(derived)* on the 70B column. NVIDIA's Jetson Benchmarks page (vLLM, JetPack 7.0, input 2048 / output 128, concurrency 1, weight dtype not stated so `format_id` is null) measures **Qwen3 30B-A3B** at **61.0** tok/s per request ([[benchmarks/nv-thor-vllm-qwen3-30b-a3b-c1-decode]]). 61.0/13.7 = **4.5×** that 20 GB floor *(derived)* (61.0 ÷ (273/20) = 4.47). The same page's concurrency-8 column is **226.4** tok/s aggregate ([[benchmarks/nv-thor-vllm-qwen3-30b-a3b-c8-aggregate]]), 226.4/8 = **28.3** per stream *(derived)* — an aggregate, not a second per-request rate, and not comparable to the 61.0.
> - **The dense rows on the same page land under the floor, which is what a dense read looks like on this bus.** Qwen3 32B decodes at **13.19** tok/s ([[benchmarks/nv-thor-vllm-qwen3-32b-c1-decode]]) against the 27B ceiling of 17.1 → 13.19/17.1 = **0.77×** *(derived)*. Llama 3.3 70B decodes at **4.7** tok/s ([[benchmarks/nv-thor-vllm-llama33-70b-c1-decode]]) against the 70B ceiling of 6.8 → 4.7/6.8 = **0.69×** *(derived)*. A 32B file is larger than the 16 GB denominator and a 70B file is larger than the 40 GB one, and the page does not state the quant, so both ratios are a lower bound on a Q4 read rather than a Q4 measurement. Neither enters §4b: the part is outside the selection rule, and the dtype is unnamed. Llama 3.1 8B at **41.3** tok/s ([[benchmarks/nv-thor-vllm-llama31-8b-c1-decode]]) is the wrong byte count for the 20 GB ceiling (41.3/13.7 = **3.0×** *(derived)*) and stays a small-model calibration.
> - **The named 35B-A3B target was already on this part, and the new row is not it.** The third-party SGLang cell already cited below is **Qwen3.6-35B-A3B FP8** at **14.6** tok/s ([[benchmarks/thor-sglang-qwen36-35b-fp8-decode]]), 14.6/13.7 = **1.07×** the same floor *(derived)*. 61.0/14.6 = **4.2×** *(derived)* between the vendor's unnamed-dtype 30B-A3B and that FP8 35B-A3B. Different model, different quant, different engine (vLLM vs SGLang), same bus — the gap is not a claim that Thor got faster, and neither number is a Q4. The 30B-A3B row does not enter the §4b proxy set for the same reason the GB10 note gives: no price, and the part is outside the selection rule. It does corroborate the direction that note already records, that an A3B on 273 GB/s sits several times above the dense-equivalent floor when the quant is not FP8.
> - **The MLPerf rows are aggregate throughput, not a decode ceiling.** v5.0 llama2-70b-99 FP16 Offline is **573.765** tok/s aggregate on one Thor ([[benchmarks/mlperf-v5-0-thor-llama2-70b-offline-tokens]], TensorRT 10.8, open division). Offline is a throughput scenario; the record says so, and 573.765 is not divided into the 6.8 tok/s per-request floor. v6.0 llama3.1-8b-edge FP4 Offline is **350.605** tok/s aggregate ([[benchmarks/mlperf-v6-0-thor-llama31-8b-edge-offline-tokens]]); the SingleStream siblings are TTFT and TPOT, not tok/s, so they are not a ceiling either. None of the 18 new rows is a purchase price, so none moves a $/tok-s rank.

The ratio of the two tg128 cells runs from **0.957×** to **1.061×** on 22 of the 23 *(derived)*: [[accelerators/nvidia-rtx-3090]] **171.61** vs **164.05** ([[benchmarks/llamacpp-vulkan-rtx-3090-llama27b-q40-fa-tg128]], [[benchmarks/llamacpp-vulkan-rtx-3090-llama27b-q40-nofa-tg128]], 171.61/164.05 = 1.046); [[accelerators/nvidia-rtx-5090]] **273.68** vs **263.63** (1.038); [[accelerators/amd-radeon-rx-7900-xtx]] **190.92** vs **182.63** (1.045); [[accelerators/nvidia-tesla-p100]] **66.48** vs **63.16** (1.053); [[accelerators/apple-m4-max]] **71.79** vs **75.02** (0.957, flash attention slower); [[accelerators/amd-radeon-rx-9070-xt]] **131.54** vs **137.11** (0.959, also slower). The one card outside that band is [[accelerators/nvidia-rtx-5070-ti]]: **176.18** with flash attention against **135.63** without ([[benchmarks/llamacpp-vulkan-rtx-5070-ti-llama27b-q40-fa-tg128]], [[benchmarks/llamacpp-vulkan-rtx-5070-ti-llama27b-q40-nofa-tg128]]), 176.18/135.63 = **1.299×** *(derived)*. Both rows are `status: verified` and both cite the same discussion page, so the split is kept rather than averaged — the same treatment the 3090 Ti's two Hardware Corner pages get. 176.18/44.8 = **3.93×** the 20 GB ceiling *(derived)* and 135.63/44.8 = **3.03×**; the 3090's own FA cell is 171.61/46.8 = **3.67×** and the 5090's is 273.68/89.6 = **3.05×**.

The pp512 siblings (for example **5,200.7** on the 3090 and **11,796.4** on the 5090) are prefill and are not a decode ceiling. > **Refresh note (docs/fit-matrix-oct14, Oct-17, this pass).** Re-ran `python tools/fit_matrix_35b.py` on local `main` `166dd51` (205 accelerator records, 218 supply records, 3,367 benchmarks — #190 on top of `b739d00`). The selection rule still yields **152 qualifying / 43 priced / 109 unpriced** (68 of the unpriced fit 35B-A3B, 41 do not). Every priced cell was re-diffed against the generator: id, VRAM, bandwidth, the three fit flags, the 35B ceiling, the supply id, the price, the derived $/tok-s, and the order all match, so **the ranked table does not move** and no rank below is renumbered. #190 adds no accelerator and no purchase price. What it adds is the depth sweep §4b said the 3090 Ti did not have, on a part the table already prices at rank 10:
>
> - **RTX 3090 Ti, rank 10, now has the 4k cell, and the new page disagrees with the old one.** [[accelerators/nvidia-rtx-3090-ti]] (24 GB, 1,008 GB/s, 35B ceiling 1,008/20 = **50.4** tok/s *(derived)*, $950 → $18.85/tok-s *(derived)* from [[supply/used-rtx-3090-ti-market-oct2026]]). Hardware Corner's March 2026 per-card llama.cpp table measures Qwen3 30B-A3B Q4_K, batch 1, at **166.9 / 121.9 / 92.0** tok/s for 4k / 16k / 32k ([[benchmarks/hc26-3090ti-qwen3-30ba3b-tg-4k]], [[benchmarks/hc26-3090ti-qwen3-30ba3b-tg-16k]], [[benchmarks/hc26-3090ti-qwen3-30ba3b-tg-32k]]). Against the 50.4 floor that is **3.3× / 2.4× / 1.8×** *(derived)* (166.9/50.4 = 3.31, 121.9/50.4 = 2.42, 92.0/50.4 = 1.83). The 16k cell already in §4b is **98.25** ([[benchmarks/hwcorner-rtx-3090-ti-qwen3-30b-a3b-q4kxl-decode-16k]]) and the 32k cell already cited is **91.97** ([[benchmarks/hwcorner-rtx-3090-ti-qwen3-30b-q4kxl-decode-32k]]). The new 32k number is 0.03 tok/s off that one; the new 16k number is **23.65 tok/s higher**. Both pages are kept, neither is averaged, and the new rows are `status: contested` in the records themselves because the same lab's ranking page and per-card page disagree on shared cells. At the new 4k cell the $950 sticker is **$5.69 per measured tok/s** *(derived, 950/166.9)*. The 3090's own verified 4k cell is 179.0 on the same $950, which is **$5.31** *(derived, 950/179.0)* — the Ti does not take the short-context measured lead, and the two 4k pages are different cards, so that gap is not a protocol split.
> - **The dense 32B row on the same card lands under the 27B ceiling.** Qwen3 32B Q4_K decodes at **38.0** tok/s at 4k and **32.6** at 16k ([[benchmarks/hc26-3090ti-qwen3-32b-tg-4k]], [[benchmarks/hc26-3090ti-qwen3-32b-tg-16k]]). The 27B ceiling is 1,008/16 = **63.0** tok/s *(derived)*, so those cells are 38.0/63.0 = **0.60×** and 32.6/63.0 = **0.52×** *(derived)*. A 32B file is larger than the 16 GB denominator, so both ratios are a lower bound on how far under the floor a dense Q4 read sits — the same shape the R9700 note above records, not a new rank.
> - **The rest of #190 is the wrong byte count for this table.** gpt-oss 20B MXFP4 on the 3090 Ti (160.3 at 4k down to 61.5 at 128k) and on the RTX 5070 Ti (156.0 at 4k down to 66.6 at 128k), plus the Qwen3 8B and 14B depth rows, are smaller than the 20 GB denominator. None of them enters the §4b proxy set and none moves a $/tok-s rank. The 5070 Ti still has no 30B-A3B cell beyond the 111.11 already quoted.

> **Refresh note (docs/fit-matrix-oct14, Oct-17, this pass).** Re-ran `python tools/fit_matrix_35b.py` on local `main` `d8214c6` (205 accelerator records, 218 supply records, 3,305 benchmarks — #186, #187 and #188 on top of `2c7fa57`). The selection rule still yields **152 qualifying / 43 priced / 109 unpriced** (68 of the unpriced fit 35B-A3B, 41 do not). Every priced cell was re-diffed against the generator: id, VRAM, bandwidth, the three fit flags, the 35B ceiling, the supply id, the price, the derived $/tok-s, and the order all match, so **the ranked table does not move** and no rank below is renumbered. #186 is a 4B model record and #187 is a services price; neither touches an accelerator, a supply row, or a benchmark. #188 adds no accelerator and no purchase price. What it adds is the first **current-generation dense 27B** decode on a part §4 already lists and cannot price:
>
> - **Radeon AI PRO R9700, unpriced, fits 35B and 27B, not 70B, and the dense row lands under the 27B ceiling.** [[accelerators/amd-radeon-ai-pro-r9700]] is 32 GB GDDR6 at **640 GB/s** (same bus as [[accelerators/amd-radeon-rx-9070-xt]], which is priced and does not fit). 35B ceiling 640/20 = **32.0** tok/s *(derived)*; 27B ceiling 640/16 = **40.0** tok/s *(derived)*. Flags stay **Y/Y/-** (32 ≥ 24, 32 ≥ 20, 32 < 48). local-llm-benchmarks.dev measures Qwen3.8-27B UD-Q4_K_XL, concurrency 1, depth 0, a 2048-token prompt and 256 generated tokens, TTFT excluded, llama.cpp 0.5.0-dev. ROCm 10.0 decodes at **30.66** tok/s ([[benchmarks/llmb-r9700-1gpu-rocm-qwen38-27b-q4kxl-d0-decode]]; repeat means 30.826 and 30.485) and prefills at **3,048.78** tok/s ([[benchmarks/llmb-r9700-1gpu-rocm-qwen38-27b-q4kxl-d0-prefill]]). Vulkan on the same host and the same file decodes at **28.39** ([[benchmarks/llmb-r9700-1gpu-vulkan-qwen38-27b-q4kxl-d0-decode]]) and prefills at **1,016.06** ([[benchmarks/llmb-r9700-1gpu-vulkan-qwen38-27b-q4kxl-d0-prefill]]). Against the 27B ceiling that is 30.66/40.0 = **0.77×** and 28.39/40.0 = **0.71×** *(derived)*. Under the floor, which is what a dense Q4 read looks like on this bus — the same shape the 3090's Qwen3.5-27B Q4_K cell already shows at 0.57× (33.5/58.5), on a different card and a different protocol, so the two ratios are not a ranking. Dividing the same ROCm decode by the 20 GB 35B denominator instead gives 30.66/32.0 = **0.96×** *(derived)*, and that division is the wrong byte count for a 27B file; it is not an A3B measurement and it does not enter §4b. The older rows on this card stay where they were: Llama-2-7B Q4_0 tg128 is a small-model calibration (Vulkan **145.67** no-flash-attention, ROCm **97.98** with flash attention), and the Q1_0 Bonsai-27B row is a 3.53 GiB file, not this Q4. No street price, so the card cannot enter §3. The ROCm-vs-Vulkan gap on one file is 30.66 vs 28.39 on decode and 3,048.78 vs 1,016.06 on the 2048-token prefill — a backend gap, not a bandwidth one.
>
> **Refresh note (docs/fit-matrix-oct14, Oct-17, prior pass).** Re-ran `python tools/fit_matrix_35b.py` on local `main` `2c7fa57` (205 accelerator records, 218 supply records, 3,301 benchmarks — #183 and #184 on top of `6180a58`). The selection rule now yields **152 qualifying / 43 priced / 109 unpriced** (68 of the unpriced fit 35B-A3B, 41 do not). Every priced cell was re-diffed against the generator: id, VRAM, bandwidth, the three fit flags, the 35B ceiling, the supply id, the price, the derived $/tok-s, and the order all match, so **the ranked table does not move** and no rank below is renumbered. #184 is a services price refresh and touches no accelerator, supply, or benchmark. #183 adds no purchase price. What it adds to the gate is four bandwidth-qualified cards that fit nothing in this doc's model class, all `-/-/-`, all unpriced, so they extend the §4 close rather than §3 or §4:
>
> - **Radeon VII, the interesting near-miss.** [[accelerators/amd-radeon-vii]] is 16 GB of HBM2 at **1,024 GB/s** — the same bus the record's notes give [[accelerators/amd-radeon-pro-vii]], and the same 35B ceiling, 1024/20 = **51.2** tok/s *(derived)*. 16 GB is under the 24 GB the 35B-A3B fit needs and under the 20 GB the 27B fit needs, so the flags stay `-/-/-` and the card does not enter §3. The record stores the page's bandwidth row and not its "4 Gbps" speed row: those two disagree (4 Gbps × 4096 bit / 8 = 2,048 GB/s), and the record leaves that disagreement in its notes rather than averaging it. llama.cpp Vulkan #10879, Llama-2-7B Q4_0, tg128 is **101.19** with flash attention ([[benchmarks/llamacpp-vulkan-radeonvii-llama27b-q40-fa-tg128]]) and **110.86** without ([[benchmarks/llamacpp-vulkan-radeonvii-llama27b-q40-nofa-tg128]]). Dividing by the 20 GB ceiling — the wrong byte count for a 3.56 GiB model — gives 101.19/51.2 = **2.0×** and 110.86/51.2 = **2.2×** *(derived)*. That is a small-model calibration, not a claim the card decodes a 35B-A3B at 100 tok/s; 16 GB cannot hold the 24 GB the fit flag requires. pp512 is 1,059.14 / 1,007.19 ([[benchmarks/llamacpp-vulkan-radeonvii-llama27b-q40-fa-pp512]], [[benchmarks/llamacpp-vulkan-radeonvii-llama27b-q40-nofa-pp512]]).
> - **Arc A770 16GB, same shape, half the bus.** [[accelerators/intel-arc-a770]] is 16 GB GDDR6 at **560 GB/s**, ceiling 560/20 = **28.0** tok/s *(derived)*, also `-/-/-`. tg128 is **52.56** with flash attention ([[benchmarks/llamacpp-vulkan-a770-llama27b-q40-fa-tg128]]) and **53.07** without ([[benchmarks/llamacpp-vulkan-a770-llama27b-q40-nofa-tg128]]) → 52.56/28.0 = **1.9×** *(derived)*, same 7B caveat. The 8 GB A770 is a different card and is not this record. pp512 is 1,073.85 / 1,119.68 ([[benchmarks/llamacpp-vulkan-a770-llama27b-q40-fa-pp512]], [[benchmarks/llamacpp-vulkan-a770-llama27b-q40-nofa-pp512]]).
> - **Vega 64 and Vega 56, 8 GB, fit nothing here.** [[accelerators/amd-radeon-rx-vega-64]] is 8 GB HBM2 at **484 GB/s**, ceiling 484/20 = **24.2** tok/s *(derived)*. [[accelerators/amd-radeon-rx-vega-56]] is 8 GB at **410 GB/s**, ceiling 410/20 = **20.5** *(derived)*. Both are under every fit threshold in §2. tg128 is **63.43** / **67.70** on the 64 ([[benchmarks/llamacpp-vulkan-vega64-llama27b-q40-fa-tg128]], [[benchmarks/llamacpp-vulkan-vega64-llama27b-q40-nofa-tg128]]) and **50.23** / **52.66** on the 56 ([[benchmarks/llamacpp-vulkan-vega56-llama27b-q40-fa-tg128]], [[benchmarks/llamacpp-vulkan-vega56-llama27b-q40-nofa-tg128]]). 63.43/24.2 = **2.6×** and 50.23/20.5 = **2.5×** *(derived)*, again against Llama-2-7B, so neither enters §4b.
> - **TITAN V does not qualify, so it is not one of the 152.** [[accelerators/nvidia-titan-v]] is 12 GB HBM2 with `memory_bandwidth_gbps: null` — the product page and the launch note print no bandwidth, and the record refuses to substitute one. Null bandwidth fails the `>= 300` arm, and there is no linked purchase-price record at `<= $3,000`, so the selection rule keeps it out. Its #10879 tg128 of **100.69** / **109.21** ([[benchmarks/llamacpp-vulkan-titanv-llama27b-q40-fa-tg128]], [[benchmarks/llamacpp-vulkan-titanv-llama27b-q40-nofa-tg128]]) is therefore not divided into a ceiling this doc does not have a bandwidth for. All twenty new rows are Llama-2-7B Q4_0, so none of them is an A3B measurement and none moves a rank.

A 7B dense model is the wrong byte count for the 20 GB ceiling, so none of them enters §4b and none moves a rank. The seven that are the workload are a second protocol on a part the table already prices:
>
> - **Strix Halo 8060S, rank 37, and the long-context column does not collapse.** [[accelerators/amd-radeon-8060s-ryzen-ai-max]] (128 GB unified, 256 GB/s, 35B ceiling 256/20 = **12.8** tok/s *(derived)*, $1,900 → $148.44/tok-s *(derived)*). The homelab page's single-run llama-bench (`-fa 1 -r 1 --mmap 0`) measures Qwen3-30B-A3B UD-Q4_K_XL under Vulkan AMDVLK 2025.Q2.1 at **81.79** tok/s at depth 0 and **85.11** at depth 130560 ([[benchmarks/shhl-8060s-qwen3-30b-a3b-udq4-amdvlk-tg128]], [[benchmarks/shhl-8060s-qwen3-30b-a3b-udq4-amdvlk-tg128-d130560]]). Against the 12.8 floor that is **6.4×** and **6.6×** *(derived)* (81.79/12.8 = 6.39, 85.11/12.8 = 6.65). The same build's tuned-ROCm column (ROCm 7.0.0-0, hipBLASLt) is **64.17** at depth 0 and **67.66** at depth 130560 ([[benchmarks/shhl-8060s-qwen3-30b-a3b-udq4-rocm-tuned-tg128]], [[benchmarks/shhl-8060s-qwen3-30b-a3b-udq4-rocm-tuned-tg128-d130560]]) → **5.0×** and **5.3×** *(derived)* (64.17/12.8 = 5.01, 67.66/12.8 = 5.29). Depth 130560 is ~128k of primed context, and both columns *hold* — AMDVLK is slightly faster at the long depth than at zero. That contradicts the 5-run wiki column already on file for the same model on the same box: AMDVLK there is 130.56 at depth 0 and **27.32** at depth 131072 ([[benchmarks/wiki-strix-8060s-qwen3-30b-a3b-udq4-amdvlk-tg128-d0]], [[benchmarks/wiki-strix-8060s-qwen3-30b-a3b-udq4-amdvlk-tg128-d128k]]), and the plain ROCm b6702 column already filed is 64.17 at depth 0 collapsing to **4.98** at depth 130560 ([[benchmarks/shhl-8060s-qwen3-30b-a3b-udq4-rocm-tg128-d130560]]). Same silicon, three protocols, three answers at ~128k (85.11, 27.32, 4.98). The single-run rows are not averaged into the 5-run ones, and they do not replace the 72.35 cell §4b ranks on ([[benchmarks/lhl-8060s-qwen3-30b-a3b-ud-q4-k-xl-vulkan-tg1]], 5.7×) or the 130.56 depth-0 row the §4b prose calls 10.2× (130.56/12.8 = 10.20, *(derived)*). What they do is bound the claim: the floor stays a floor at 128k on this build, and the collapse §4b warns about is a backend effect, not a bandwidth one. $1,900/85.11 = **$22.32 per measured tok/s** *(derived)* at depth 130560 — still above the 3090's $5.31 at its 4k cell, and a different context depth, so it does not re-rank. pp512 on the new AMDVLK column is 741.6 at depth 0 and 755.14 at depth 130560 ([[benchmarks/shhl-8060s-qwen3-30b-a3b-udq4-amdvlk-pp512]], [[benchmarks/shhl-8060s-qwen3-30b-a3b-udq4-amdvlk-pp512-d130560]]); the tuned-ROCm pp512 at depth 130560 is 659.07 ([[benchmarks/shhl-8060s-qwen3-30b-a3b-udq4-rocm-tuned-pp512-d130560]]). The depth-0 tuned pp512 of 651.93 was already filed and was not duplicated.

What it does add is a bandwidth that was previously null: [[accelerators/furiosaai-rngd-renegade]] moved from `memory_bandwidth_gbps: null` / `vram_gb: null` to **48 GB HBM3 at 1,500 GB/s** (vendor product page, cited on the record). 48 ≥ 24, 48 ≥ 20 and 48 ≥ 48, so the fit flags are **Y/Y/Y**; the 35B ceiling is 1500/20 = **75.0 tok/s** *(derived)*, which slots it in §4 between [[accelerators/biren-br100]] (80.0) and [[accelerators/huawei-ascend-910b]] (70.0). It stays unpriced: the only Furiosa supply row, [[supply/furiosaai-rngd-eval-only-channel]], has `price_usd: null`, `kind: oem-direct`, and its `accelerator_ids` still point at the Warboy, not the RNGD.
